38: ## B. New items (Claude v2/v3 delta review)
39: 
40: **C14 — closes the MAJOR proof gap; proof-k6-lemmas-v1 §3, Step 2.** The proof asserts that "τ involves only the primes 2 and 3". Here is the derivation that was missing.
41: 
42: - **Setting.** Take a triple j, k, l ≠ v. All three α lie in {2, 4}, and at most one of the triangle's edges is opposite the E2 vertex.
43: - **Bounds on τ.**
44:   - τ_min ∈ {2, 4}.
45:   - τ_max = 4, 8 or 16, according as at most one, exactly two, or all three edges equal 4.
46:   - With two or more 4-edges, at least one 4-edge is not opposite E2, so τ_min = 4.
47: - **Admissible τ_jkl.** These are the multiples of τ_min that are at most τ_max:
48: 
49:   | triangle | admissible τ_jkl |
50:   |---|---|
51:   | (2,2,2) | {2, 4} |
52:   | (2,2,4), with E2 opposite the 4-edge | {2, 4} |
53:   | (2,2,4), otherwise | {4} |
54:   | (2,4,4) | {4, 8} |
55:   | (4,4,4) | {4, 8, 12, 16} |
56: 
57: - **Consequences.**
58:   - gcd(r_v, τ_jkl) = 1 whenever r_v is odd and 3 ∤ r_v. This covers r_v = 1, v = E5 (where 5 | r_v), primes ≥ 7 and arbitrary prime powers.
59:   - 3 | τ_jkl happens only when τ = 12 in a (4,4,4) triangle.
60:   - Hence hypothesis (iv) holds automatically for v ≠ E3. For v = E3, the test of C11 holds exactly when there is no all-4 triangle among the neighbours.
61: - **Placement.** Insert this paragraph before "gcd(r_v, r_lτ_jkl) = …" in Step 2.
62: 
63: **C15 — rules-k6-v2 L9(a) and proof-k6-lemmas-v1 §1.**
64: - In the statement of L9(a), add the hypothesis "H < G whenever |T| = 6 is used".
65: - In proof §1, the phrase "assume H < G if |S| = 6 is used" should read "|T| = 6".
66: - With H = G, the case |T| = 6 would be Sun(6) for G itself. That would be circular, and it is excluded.
67: 
68: **C16 — saturation for FH5, including the prime 5.** In setting (a), fgcd equals the true gcd of λ_l = r_lt_l:
69: - common primes lie in {2, 3, 5};
70: - class-1 E2 is exact;
71: - class-2 E2: v₂(t) ≤ 2 ≤ v₂(r);
72: - E3: v₃(t) ≤ 1 ≤ v₃(r);
73: - **E5: v₅(t) ≤ 1 ≤ v₅(r_E5)**.
74: 
75: **C17 — which allowed family each dual respects.**
76: - Engine-v1 RLP is not used for certificates. Its notion of "allowed" is the rules-k6-v1 family.
77: - Engine v2 and v3 RLP and RPLP use the hereditary family of rules-k6-v2 L9.
78: - Both families are sound.
79: 
80: **C18 — closure wording.**
81: - The certificate set rules out a Core5(6) configuration **in a minimal-order Sun(6) counterexample**. FH5 may use Sun(6) inside H < G, and this is why the restriction is needed.
82: - R1(6) then gives Sun(6). Unrestricted Core5(6) follows from Sun(6).
83: 
84: **C19 — written obligation that the markers not in the type are irrelevant.** E5 is not part of the type. The rules K, P, RLP, RPLP and N26 do not depend on E5:
85: - no α and no q is divisible by 5;
86: - the cofactors share no prime;
87: - for N26, see C14 (the case v = E5 is covered).
88: 
89: **C20 — publication of the pruned-node count.** run.json publishes `pruned_nodes`. That count depends on the engine's search order and is informational only. The checker must recompute leaves and orbits; see CERT-FORMAT-k6-v3 §6.
90: 
91: **C21 — an optional strengthening, NOT used.** XE bars the equality X_j = X_k whenever α_jk ≥ 2. So in P and RPLP, hi could drop by u even when both K2 conditions hold. No engine uses this.
92: 
93: **C22 — the checker's fail-closed clauses.** These are fixed in CERT-FORMAT-k6-v3 §6.

SOURCE 02-CERT-FORMAT-v3.md SHA256 fd3223168a32887100f1b17198bc2dc94d3cc9b28df306871f1c254b6c7fd62f ORIGINAL /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/sun-coset6/CERT-FORMAT-k6-v3.md
1: # CERT-FORMAT-k6-v3: self-contained certificate format for the Core5(6) case analysis (supersedes v1 and v2 texts; the certificates are unchanged)
2: 
3: This file describes the output of `code/core56_engine_v3.py` (sha256 01f0de4c…d0cd), run under rules-k6-v1, v2 and v3. It is written so that an independent checker can be built from this file plus the rules texts, without reading any engine code.
4: 
5: The host-clock freeze record is in `evidence/erratum-v3.sha256`. The certificates are produced in `evidence/engine-v3-core56/` and are unchanged.
6: 
7: **Changes from v2 (26b1dbcc…854c)**, following the Claude v2/v3 delta review:
8: - SAT is now specified for the primes 2, 3 and 5.
9: - §4 states the corrected closure.
10: - A new §6 lists the fail-closed clauses that every checker must enforce.
11: 
12: **Changes in v2 relative to v1:** explicit rounding for PB, and the corrected N26 test for E3.
13: 
14: ## 0. Mathematical setting (summary of rules-k6-v1 §0)
15: 
16: **The configuration.**
17: - G is a finite group. There are six subgroups U_0, …, U_5 with [G:U_i] = 5r_i, and the r_i are pairwise coprime.
18: - The cosets g_iU_i are pairwise disjoint.
19: - For i ≠ j, put α_ij = [G:U_i∩U_j]/(5r_ir_j). Each α_ij lies in {1, 2, 3, 4}.
20: - A pattern is the symmetric matrix α together with the special statuses below.
21: 
22: **Special statuses.**
23: - **E2:** the vertex with r even, if any, together with a class: c2 = 1 if v₂(r) = 1, and c2 = 2 if v₂(r) ≥ 2.
24: - **E3:** the vertex with 3 | r, if any.
25: - **E5:** the vertex with 5 | r, if any. It is used only by FH5, which must refute all seven placements: none, or each of the vertices 0..5.
26: 
27: **Units.** All sizes are in units of |G|.
28: 
29: ## 1. Types and stabilisers
30: 
31: **The eight types.** A type is a triple (e2, c2, e3). Here e2 is either `None` or the E2 vertex, c2 ∈ {0, 1, 2} with 0 meaning "no E2", and e3 is either `None` or the E3 vertex. The types used are exactly:
32: 
33: ```
34: (None,0,None) (None,0,0) (0,1,None) (0,1,0) (0,1,1) (0,2,None) (0,2,0) (0,2,1)
35: ```
36: 
37: - (0,c,0) means E2 and E3 lie on the same vertex 0.
38: - (0,c,1) means they lie on different vertices.
39: 
40: **Completeness.** Every placement of E2 (with its class) and E3 on the six vertices is mapped onto exactly one of these types by some permutation in S6.
41: 
42: **Stabiliser of a type.** These are the permutations p of {0, …, 5} with p(e2) = e2 when e2 is not `None`, and p(e3) = e3 when e3 is not `None`. E2 and E3 are never exchanged. Orbits of a type are orbits of this group acting on α-labellings.
43: 
44: **String keys.** In run.json, types appear as Python tuple strings, for example `"(None, 0, None)"` and `"(0, 2, 1)"`.
45: 
46: ## 2. Edge order and leaf codes
47: 
48: **Edge order E.** This order is used for every 15-character code:
49: 
50: ```
51: (0,1),(0,2),(1,2),(0,3),(1,3),(2,3),(0,4),(1,4),(2,4),(3,4),(0,5),(1,5),(2,5),(3,5),(4,5)
52: ```
53: 
54: These are the pairs (i,j) with i < j, sorted by (j, i).
55: 
56: **Codes.** A labelling is the 15-character string of its α-values, each in {1, 2, 3, 4}, written in edge order E.
57: 
58: **T-consistent leaves.** For a fixed type, a labelling is a *T-consistent leaf* if every one of the 20 triangles passes rule T (rules-k6-v1 L1).
59: 
60: - For each edge e of a triangle, take the vertex z opposite e and the table entry gcd(α_e, r_z):
61:   - α = 1: 1.
62:   - α = 2: 2 if z = E2, else 1.
63:   - α = 3: 3 if z = E3, else 1.
64:   - α = 4: 4 if z = E2 with c2 = 2; 2 if z = E2 with c2 = 1; else 1.
65: - **τ_min** is the lcm over the three edges e of α_e / gcd(α_e, r_z).
66: - **τ_max** is the minimum, over the three vertices, of the product of the two α's at that vertex.
67: - **T passes** if τ_min ≤ τ_max.
68: 
69: Every labelling that fails T on some triangle is killed by that triangle, by rules-k6-v1 L1. No further certificate is needed for it.
70: 
71: **What run.json reports per type** (under `summary[type]`):
72: - `leaves_labelled`: the number of T-consistent leaves.
73: - `pruned_nodes`: the number of pruned nodes. This count belongs to the engine's search order and does not need to be matched.
74: - `leafcodes_sha256`: the sha256 of the UTF-8 string formed by sorting all T-consistent leaf codes ascending, joining them with "\n", and appending a final "\n".
75: - `orbits`: the number of stabiliser-orbits of T-consistent leaves.
76: - `orbit_rule_counts`: counts by rule.
77: 
78: ## 3. orbits.jsonl.gz
79: 
80: The file is gzip-compressed. It has one JSON object per line, and one line for every orbit of every type:
81: 
82: ```
83: {"type":[e2,c2,e3], "code":"<engine canonical code>", "rep":"<15-char labelled representative, edge order E>",
84:  "rule":"K|RLP|PB|Ppair|FH5|N26|RPLP|SURVIVE", "witness":[...]}   (no "witness" key when rule is SURVIVE)
85: ```
86: 
87: - `code` is the engine's canonical form. A checker should use its own canonical form and check that the representatives (`rep`) meet each orbit exactly once.
88: - The rule fields below all refer to `rep`, read as a labelled pattern of the given type.
89: 
90: ### "K", witness `[v, S]`
91: 
92: This is rules-k6-v1 L3 with K1.
93: - **Check:** Σ_{i∈S} α_iv ≥ 5, and τ_min(i,v,j) = α_iv·α_vj for every pair {i,j} ⊆ S. Here τ_min is computed on the triangle {i, v, j} as in §2.
94: - **Proves:** the pattern is impossible, because L3 requires Σ ≤ 4.
95: 
96: ### "RLP", witness `[v]`
97: 
98: This is rules-k6-v1 L7 with rules-k6-v2 L9.
99: 
100: **Neighbours.** Write N for the five vertices other than v, in ascending order. For each j ∈ N, set st_j = (E2-class digit, E3 flag). The class digit is 0 if j is not E2 and c2 if j is E2. The flag is 1 if j = E3 and 0 otherwise.
101: 
102: **Admissible q.** Every q ∈ ∏_{j∈N} {1, …, 5 − α_vj}, enumerated in itertools.product order with the first neighbour varying slowest.
103: 
104: **Allowed supports S** (S is a non-empty subset of positions 0..4 of N; every subset must be considered):
105: 1. **Exponents.** For each position x: e2(x) = v₂(q_x) + {0 if digit 0; 1 if digit 1; SAT₂ if digit 2}, and e3(x) = v₃(q_x) + {SAT₃ if flag 1}. Here SAT₂ ≥ 3 and SAT₃ ≥ 2 suffice for RLP and RPLP; any larger value, e.g. 30, gives the same gcds (saturation, erratum-v3 C5/C16).
106: 2. **maxgcd.** maxgcd(x,y) = 2^min(e2(x),e2(y)) · 3^min(e3(x),e3(y)).
107: 3. **Allowed.** S is allowed if |S| = 1, or if every subset T ⊆ S with |T| ≥ 2 contains a pair with maxgcd ≥ |T|.
108: 
109: **The dual.** In `rlp_duals.json`, under key = `"q_1,...,q_5|d1f1;d2f2;...;d5f5"` (digits of st_j in neighbour order), there must be y = list of 5 exact rationals as strings such that:
110: - y_x ≥ 0 for every x;
111: - Σ_{x∈S} y_x ≤ 1 for **every** allowed S;
112: - Σ_x (q_x/5)·y_x ≥ 1.
113: 
114: **Proves:** for every admissible q, the covering LP minimum is ≥ 1 by weak duality. So vertex v is impossible.
115: 
116: `value` in `rlp_duals.json` is informational. The check is the inequalities above.
117: 
118: ### "PB", witness `[v, S]`, and "Ppair", witness `[v, [j,k]]`
119: 
120: This is rules-k6-v1 L4.
121: 
122: **Per pair** {j,k} ⊆ N, sizes in units of |G|:
123: - x_j = α_vj/5.
124: - u = 1/(5·gcd(r_v, α_jk)), with the gcd read from the §2 table taking z = v.
125: - **lo:** let Lb = max(τ_min(v,j,k)/(5α_jk), x_j + x_k − 1, 0). Then lo = u·⌈Lb/u⌉, the least multiple of u that is ≥ Lb.
126: - **hi:** start from hi = u·⌊min(x_j, x_k)/u⌋, the greatest multiple of u that is ≤ min(x_j, x_k). If hi = min(x_j, x_k), lower it by one u when the containment is barred:
127:   - if x_j < x_k, barred unless α_vj + α_kj ≤ 4;
128:   - if x_k < x_j, barred unless α_vk + α_jk ≤ 4;
129:   - if x_j = x_k, barred unless both inequalities hold.
130: 
131: **Ppair:** check lo > hi for the witnessed pair.
132: 
133: **PB:** every pair of N has lo ≤ hi (otherwise a Ppair would apply), and
134:   Σ_{j∈S}(1 − x_j) − Σ_{{j,k}⊆S}(1 − x_j − x_k + hi_jk) ≥ 1.
135: 
136: **Proves:** the pattern is impossible.
137: 
138: ### "FH5", witness `[[a,b], V, counts]`
139: 
140: This is rules-k6-v1 L5 and L6 with rules-k6-v2 L9.
141: 
142: **Hypotheses and setup:**
143: - α_ab = 1, and V = {v ∉ {a,b} : α_va = α_vb} is non-empty.
144: - There are m = 5 cosets, labelled 0..4.
145: 
146: **Coset choices for each vertex l:**
147: - l = a: fixed to t = 1 and coset set {0}.
148: - l = b, or l ∈ V with α_la = 1: t = 1, with each of the 5 singletons.
149: - l ∈ V with α_la = c > 1: t = c, with all C(5,c) subsets.
150: - Every other vertex: t ∈ {1..5}, with all t-subsets.
151: 
152: **Rejection rules.** An assignment is rejected iff (i) or (ii) holds:
153: - (i) (F3) fails: some vertex i with t_i = 1 has α_il > t_l for some l ≠ i.
154: - (ii) Some coset c has a member set M_c = {l : c ∈ set_l} of size ≥ 2 that contains a subset T, |T| ≥ 2, in which no pair has fgcd ≥ |T|.
155: 
156: Here fgcd(x,y) = 2^min · 3^min · 5^min of the exponents of λ_l = r_l·t_l:
157: - 2-exponent: v₂(t) + {1 if l = E2 with c2 = 1; SAT if l = E2 with c2 = 2};
158: - 3-exponent: v₃(t) + {SAT if l = E3};
159: - 5-exponent: v₅(t) + {SAT₅ if l = e5}, where **SAT₅ ≥ 1** is required. In FH5, SAT₂ ≥ 2, SAT₃ ≥ 1 and SAT₅ ≥ 1 are required. The value 30 is safe for all three. With SAT₅ = 0 a checker would underestimate fgcd and reject an assignment that may be real, which is unsound.
160: 
161: **The certificate.** Every assignment is rejected, for each of the seven placements e5 ∈ {None, 0, …, 5}. `counts` lists the number of assignments examined per placement. These counts are informational and depend on the engine's loop order.
162: 
163: ### "N26", witness `[v]`
164: 
165: This is rules-k6-v3 L11. The check:
166: - v ≠ E2;
167: - α_vj = 2 for all j ≠ v;
168: - α_jk ∈ {2, 4} for all pairs j, k ≠ v;
169: - If v = E3: τ_max(j,k,l) < 3·τ_min(j,k,l) for **every** triple j, k, l ≠ v.
170:   - Why this suffices: every α among the neighbours lies in {2, 4}, so every τ_min is a power of 2, and τ is a multiple of τ_min that is ≤ τ_max.
171:   - The possible τ by triangle type are listed below. "At most one 4-edge" means that at most one 4-edge has its divisibility reduced by E2.
172: 
173:     | triangle | τ_min | possible τ |
174:     |---|---|---|
175:     | 2,2,2 | 2 | {2, 4} |
176:     | 2,2,4 | 2 or 4 | {2, 4} or {4} |
177:     | 2,4,4 | 4 | {4, 8} |
178:     | 4,4,4 | 4 | {4, 8, 12, 16} |
179: 
180:   - So the only τ divisible by 3 is 12, in a 4,4,4 triangle. The test fails exactly for such triangles: τ_max = 16 ≥ 12 = 3·τ_min.
181: 
182: ### "RPLP", witness `[v, certs]`
183: 
184: This is rules-k6-v3 L12.
185: 
186: **Shape of `certs`.** It has one entry per admissible q at v, in the itertools.product order defined under RLP.
187: - **["R", key]:** key is the RLP key for this q, and its dual must pass the RLP check above.
188: - **["P", vec]:** vec is a list of 30 exact rationals as strings, giving (a_j for j ∈ N ascending; b_j for j ∈ N ascending; u_p for pairs p of N in lexicographic order; l_p in the same pair order). The checks below apply.
189: 
190: **Exponents and allowed S** are as for RLP, with q.
191: 
192: **Forced containments.** These are the pairs (w,j) of N with τ_min(v,w,j) ≥ α_vw·α_wj.
193: 
194: **Columns.** All (A, S) such that:
195: - A ⊆ N is non-empty;
196: - no forced pair (w,j) has j ∈ A and w ∉ A;
197: - S ⊆ A, where S is empty, a singleton, or hereditary-allowed.
198: 
199: **Checks for ["P", vec]:**
200: - u_p ≥ 0 and l_p ≥ 0.
201: - **Every column:** Σ_{j∈S} a_j + Σ_{j∈A} b_j − Σ_{p⊆A} u_p + Σ_{p⊆A} l_p ≤ 1.
202: - **Objective:** Σ_j a_j(q_j/5) + Σ_j b_j(5 − α_vj)/5 − Σ_p u_p·yhi_p + Σ_p l_p·ylo_p ≥ 1, where for p = {j,k}:
203:   - yhi_p = 1 − (α_vj + α_vk)/5 + hi_jk;
204:   - ylo_p = 1 − (α_vj + α_vk)/5 + lo_jk;
205:   - lo and hi are as defined under PB.
206: 
207: **Proves:** for every admissible q, the joint LP minimum is ≥ 1, or the LP is infeasible (weak duality, or Farkas). So vertex v is impossible.
208: 
209: ### "SURVIVE"
210: 
211: No rule killed the orbit. Such orbits are listed in survivors.json; the expected number is 0.
212: 
213: ## 4. What the whole certificate set proves
214: 
215: **Statement.** Every pattern of Core5(6) is equivalent under S6 to a T-consistent leaf of one of the 8 types, or is killed by T. Every orbit of T-consistent leaves of each type has exactly one line with a valid certificate (§6).
216: 
217: **Conclusion (corrected, erratum-v3 C18).** If every line verifies under the §6 clauses and there is no SURVIVE line, then **no minimal-order Sun(6) counterexample carries a Core5(6) configuration.**
218: - **Why the minimality restriction.** FH5 may use Sun(6) inside a proper subgroup H < G of such a counterexample.
219: - **From there to Sun(6).** R1(6) (README §2; erratum-v3 C1, C12) shows that every minimal counterexample would carry a Core5(6) configuration. So Sun(6) holds for every group.
220: - **Unrestricted Core5(6).** This then follows from Sun(6).
221: 
222: **What the conclusion rests on.**
223: - The written proofs of the rules (rules-k6-v1, v2, v3; proof-k6-lemmas-v1; erratum-v3, which includes the C14 τ table for N26).
224: - The evaluation-point lemma rules-k6-v1 L8 (M1, M2), with the saturation argument of C5 and C16.
225: - The fact that E5 does not affect K, P, RLP, RPLP or N26 (C19).
226: - The frame inputs:
227:   - Sun(k ≤ 4), from Zhu;
228:   - Sun(5), from the accepted Core(5) record (R/sun-coset5-parent-verdict-v1.md, 2cf02436…);
229:   - Sun(6) in proper subgroups, by minimality. This is used only when 6 vertices meet one fibre coset in FH5.
230: 
231: ## 6. Fail-closed clauses: every checker MUST enforce these
232: 
233: These follow the Claude v2/v3 delta review, §6. If any clause fails, the whole certificate set is rejected.
234: 
235: 1. **Universe.**
236:    - For each of the 8 types, enumerate the T-consistent leaves independently, pruning only at completed triangles.
237:    - Compute the orbits under the stabiliser (fix e2 and e3; never exchange them).
238:    - Require that the representatives in `orbits.jsonl.gz` meet every orbit **exactly once**: exact equality of the orbit union, with no missing and no duplicate orbit. Matching `leafcodes_sha256` or the counts is only a cross-check.
239:    - Every line's `type` must be one of the 8 types, and its `rep` must be a T-consistent leaf of that type.
240: 2. **Well-formedness.**
241:    - Every vertex index lies in 0..5.
242:    - In K and PB, S is a duplicate-free subset of N = {0..5}∖{v}.
243:    - In Ppair, j ≠ k and both lie in N.
244:    - Each RLP y has length 5, and each RPLP vector has length 30.
245:    - Every rational is parsed exactly from its string. Floats are rejected.
246:    - JSON objects with duplicate keys are rejected, both in `rlp_duals.json` and in each line.
247: 3. **FH5.**
248:    - **Inputs.** Recompute V = {v ∉ {a,b} : α_va = α_vb} and check it equals the witnessed V. A witness V containing a vertex with α_va ≠ α_vb must be rejected. Check α_ab = 1 and a ≠ b.
249:    - **SAT values.** Use SAT₂ ≥ 2, SAT₃ ≥ 1 and SAT₅ ≥ 1.
250:    - **Enumeration.** Enumerate every assignment allowed by §3 FH5, for all 7 e5 placements. Only a's coset is fixed. The `counts` field is informational.
251: 4. **RLP and the "R" references inside RPLP.**
252:    - For every admissible q, recompute the key from (q, st) and require that it is present.
253:    - For an ["R", key] entry, require that key equals the key recomputed from the current q.
254:    - Recompute the hereditary-allowed family over all 31 non-empty subsets, and check y on every member.
255: 5. **RPLP.**
256:    - Require len(certs) = ∏_{j∈N}(5 − α_vj), with entries in the itertools.product order.
257:    - Forced pairs (w, j) are **ordered**: the condition is τ_min(v,w,j) ≥ α_vw·α_wj, with the product taken at w. Test (w, j) and (j, w) separately.
258:    - Rebuild all columns, lo, hi, yhi and ylo yourself.
259: 6. **N26.** Use the corrected test: if v = E3, then τ_max < 3·τ_min on every triple of neighbours. The "3 | τ_min" disjunct is deleted.
260: 7. **K and P.** Recompute every quantity from the representative. Use the explicit rounding for lo and hi from §3, then apply the K2 decrement.

SOURCE 03-new-lemma-proofs.md SHA256 1fded4dfaf7ebb0981881b0a697a081b8384abfdc9c9d3fa5b6f528792fd7573 ORIGINAL /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/sun-coset6/proof-k6-lemmas-v1.md
1: # proof-k6-lemmas-v1: written proofs of the k = 6 lemmas added after rules-k6-v1
2: 
3: This file covers rules-k6-v2 L9 and rules-k6-v3 L10, L11 and L12.
4: 
5: - **Freeze record.** The host-clock time and sha256 are in `evidence/proofs-v1.sha256`.
6: - **Status.** [NEW] means only "written in this lane"; it is not a novelty claim. Nothing here is a theorem claim about k = 6. The proofs are unreviewed. The parent will run hostile reviews of the v2 and v3 changes.
7: 
8: ## 0. Standing setting and imports
9: 
10: **Setting.** These hypotheses hold throughout.
11: - G is a finite group with subgroups U_0, …, U_5.
12: - [G:U_i] = 5r_i, where r_i ≥ 1 and the r_i are pairwise coprime. The case r_i = 1 is allowed, and so are arbitrary prime powers.
13: - The cosets g_0U_0, …, g_5U_5 are pairwise disjoint.
14: - The subgroups need not be distinct. In Core5(6), two equal subgroups force both cofactors to equal 1.
15: 
16: **Markers.** E2 is the vertex with r even, together with its class c2 (v₂(r) = 1, or v₂(r) ≥ 2). E3 is the vertex with 3 | r. E5 is the vertex with 5 | r. Each marker may be absent. Any two of them, or all three, may coincide.
17: 
18: **Measures.** All sizes are normalised: for X ⊆ G, write μ(X) = |X|/|G|. Then:
19: - μ(U_iU_j) = α_ij/5, where α_ij = [G:U_i∩U_j]/(5r_ir_j) ∈ {1, 2, 3, 4};
20: - μ(U_i) = 1/(5r_i).
21: 
22: **Imports** (rules-k6-v1 §0), with H a subgroup:
23: - **Sun(2):** L0.6, the Chinese remainder theorem for coprime indices.
24: - **Sun(3) and Sun(4):** Zhu Theorem 1.1.
25: - **Sun(5):** the accepted Core(5) result, `R/sun-coset5-parent-verdict-v1.md`, which binds sun-coset5 proof-v4 and its cumulative errata. Via R1, this gives Sun(5) in every group.
26: - **Sun(6):** used **only** inside a proper subgroup H < G of a minimal-order counterexample, and only in F2 when six vertices meet one H-coset (FH5). It is never used in R4′, RLP or RPLP, where at most five pieces lie in one right U_v-coset.
27: 
28: ## 1. rules-k6-v2 L9: the hereditary Sun condition
29: 
30: **Statement.** Two settings are covered.
31: - **(a) Left cosets of H.** Let H ≤ G, and assume H < G if |S| = 6 is used. Let C be a left coset of H, and let S be the set of l whose coset g_lU_l meets C. Put λ_l = [H : H∩U_l].
32: - **(b) Right cosets of U_v.** Let v be a vertex and translate so that g_v = 1. Let C = U_vy be a right coset, and let S be the set of j ≠ v with C ⊆ D_j = U_vg_jU_j. Put d_j = [U_v : U_v ∩ wU_jw⁻¹], where w ∈ g_jU_j ∩ C; the value does not depend on the choice of w.
33: 
34: In both settings: for every T ⊆ S with |T| ≥ 2, some pair l ≠ l′ in T has gcd(λ_l, λ_l′) ≥ |T| (respectively gcd(d_l, d_l′) ≥ |T|).
35: 
36: **Proof of (a).**
37: 1. **Pieces are cosets of H∩U_l.** Fix z ∈ C. For l ∈ S choose y_l ∈ g_lU_l ∩ C. Then g_lU_l = y_lU_l and C = y_lH, so g_lU_l ∩ C = y_l(U_l∩H).
38: 2. **Move into H.** Left-multiplying by z⁻¹ gives z⁻¹y_l(U_l∩H). Here z⁻¹y_l ∈ H, so this is a left coset in H of the subgroup U_l∩H, which has index λ_l in H.
39: 3. **Disjointness.** For l ≠ l′ the sets g_lU_l ∩ C and g_l′U_l′ ∩ C are disjoint, because g_lU_l ∩ g_l′U_l′ = ∅. Left translation preserves disjointness.
40: 4. **Apply Sun.** For T ⊆ S, the cosets {z⁻¹y_l(U_l∩H) : l ∈ T} are |T| pairwise disjoint left cosets in H of finite-index subgroups. Apply Sun(|T|) in H:
41:    - |T| ≤ 5: by the imports;
42:    - |T| = 6: by minimality, since H < G.
43: 
44:    So some pair has gcd ≥ |T|.
45: 
46: **Proof of (b).**
47: 1. **Pieces are right cosets.** For j ∈ S, the coset C lies in D_j, so it meets g_jU_j (rules-k6-v1 L7 R3). Choose w_j ∈ g_jU_j ∩ C. Then g_jU_j = w_jU_j and C = U_vw_j, so g_jU_j ∩ C = (U_v ∩ w_jU_jw_j⁻¹)w_j =: A_jw_j.
48: 2. **Index of A_j.** [U_v : A_j] = |U_v·w_jU_jw_j⁻¹| / |U_j|. Right multiplication by w_j preserves size, so this equals |U_vw_jU_j| / |U_j| = |D_j|/|U_j| = d_j.
49: 3. **Move into U_v.** Fix w_0 ∈ C. Right-multiplying by w_0⁻¹ gives A_jc_j with c_j = w_jw_0⁻¹ ∈ U_v.
50: 4. **Invert.** Inversion turns A_jc_j into c_j⁻¹A_j. These are pairwise disjoint left cosets in U_v, of indices d_j, because the g_jU_j are pairwise disjoint and both operations are bijections.
51: 5. **Apply Sun.** For T ⊆ S, |T| ≤ 5. Apply Sun(|T|) in U_v by the imports (Sun(6) is not needed). So some pair has gcd ≥ |T|. ∎
52: 
53: **Consequences for the rules.**
54: 
55: | Rule | Consequence |
56: |---|---|
57: | FH5 | In every coset, every subfamily T of the members passes the test. An assignment that fails it for some T is impossible. |
58: | RLP / RPLP | A support S carries positive mass only if it is **hereditary-allowed**: every T ⊆ S with \|T\| ≥ 2 has a pair with gcd ≥ \|T\|. |
59: 
60: The allowed family is therefore closed under taking subsets.
61: 
62: **Replacing gcd by maxgcd is safe.** The allowed test is monotone, i.e. upward closed, in each pairwise gcd. The true gcd(q_jr_j, q_kr_k) is at most maxgcd, which is computed from the markers:
63: - only the primes 2 and 3 can be common, since q ≤ 4 and the r's are coprime;
64: - v₂ of the pair is min(v₂(q_j) + ε_j, v₂(q_k) + ε_k), where ε = 1 for E2 of class 1, ε = SAT for E2 of class 2, and ε = 0 otherwise. Likewise for 3 with E3.
65: 
66: Each minimum **saturates at the partner's q-part**: v₂(q) ≤ 2 and v₃(q) ≤ 1. So maxgcd equals the true gcd for every instance with the given markers. No allowed support is lost.
67: 
68: ## 2. rules-k6-v3 L10 (XE)
69: 
70: **Statement.** Let v, j, k be distinct, and suppose U_vU_j = U_vU_k. Then:
71: - α_jk = 1;
72: - W := U_jU_k is a subgroup of index 5;
73: - [U_v : U_v∩W] = α_vj.
74: 
75: **Proof.**
76: 1. **X absorbs W.** X := U_vU_j satisfies XU_j = X, and since X = U_vU_k, also XU_k = X. Hence XW = X for W := ⟨U_j, U_k⟩.
77: 2. **X = U_vW.** Because 1 ∈ X, we have U_v ⊆ X, so U_vW ⊆ XW = X. Conversely, X = U_vU_j ⊆ U_vW.
78: 3. **W ≠ G.** μ(X) = α_vj/5 ≤ 4/5 < 1.
79: 4. **Index 5.** U_j ≤ W and U_k ≤ W, so [G:W] divides [G:U_j] = 5r_j and [G:U_k] = 5r_k. Hence it divides gcd(5r_j, 5r_k) = 5, and since W ≠ G, [G:W] = 5.
80: 5. **α_jk = 1.** U_jU_k ⊆ W, so α_jk/5 = μ(U_jU_k) ≤ μ(W) = 1/5. Hence α_jk = 1 and μ(U_jU_k) = μ(W), so U_jU_k = W.
81: 6. **The index of U_v∩W.** μ(U_vW) = [U_v : U_v∩W]·μ(W), by the product formula. Since μ(U_vW) = μ(X) = α_vj/5, we get [U_v : U_v∩W] = α_vj. ∎
82: 
83: **Corollary.** If α_jk ≥ 2, then U_vU_j ≠ U_vU_k for every vertex v.
84: 
85: ## 3. rules-k6-v3 L11 (N2-6)
86: 
87: **Hypotheses.** Let v be a vertex such that:
88: - (i) v ≠ E2, so r_v is odd;
89: - (ii) α_vj = 2 for all j ≠ v;
90: - (iii) α_jk ∈ {2, 4} for all distinct j, k ≠ v;
91: - (iv) for every triple {j,k,l} ⊆ V∖{v}, gcd(r_v, τ_jkl) = 1.
92: 
93: Condition (iv) holds automatically if v ≠ E3, because every prime factor of τ is 2 or 3. If v = E3, it holds when 3 ∤ τ_jkl for every triple. A sufficient test for that: for each triple, either 3 | τ_min(jkl) — impossible here, since τ_min is a product of 2-powers when all α ∈ {2,4} — or τ_max(jkl) < 3τ_min(jkl). The second suffices because τ is a multiple of τ_min that is at most τ_max.
94: 
95: **Conclusion.** No configuration with these data exists.
96: 
97: **Proof.** Translate so that g_v = 1, and put X_j = U_vU_j for j ≠ v. Then μ(X_j) = 2/5, and U_v ⊆ X_j.
98: 
99: **Step 1: μ(X_j∩X_k) = 1/5 for all j ≠ k.**
100: - (T1) for the edge jk, whose opposite vertex is v, gives α_jk | r_vτ_vjk. Since r_v is odd and α_jk ∈ {2, 4}, this gives α_jk | τ_vjk, so τ_vjk ≥ α_jk.
101: - By (T3) at apex v, Z_jk := U_v(U_j∩U_k) has μ(Z_jk) = τ_vjk/(5α_jk) ≥ 1/5. Also Z_jk ⊆ X_j∩X_k.
102: - By L0.5, X_j∩X_k is a union of (U_v, U_j∩U_k)-double cosets. By L0.4 each has μ a multiple of 1/gcd(5r_v, 5r_jr_kα_jk) = 1/(5·gcd(r_v, α_jk)) = 1/5, since r_v is odd and α_jk ∈ {2, 4}.
103: - By the XE corollary, X_j ≠ X_k. As both have measure 2/5, this gives μ(X_j∩X_k) < 2/5.
104: - Hence μ(X_j∩X_k) = 1/5, and so Z_jk = X_j∩X_k with μ(Z_jk) = 1/5.
105: 
106: **Step 2: the three Z's of a triple coincide.** Fix a triple j, k, l of vertices other than v, and put W := U_j∩U_k∩U_l.
107: - Index of W: [G:W] = 5r_jr_kr_lτ_jkl, using the definition of τ.
108: - Put Q := [G : U_v∩W]. Then Q is a multiple of [G:W] = 5r_jr_k·(r_lτ_jkl) and of T_vjk = 5r_jr_k·(r_vτ_vjk). So it is a multiple of 5r_jr_k·lcm(r_lτ_jkl, r_vτ_vjk).
109: - **The coprimality step.** The lcm is a multiple of lcm(r_lτ_jkl, r_v). This equals r_lτ_jkl·r_v, because gcd(r_v, r_lτ_jkl) = gcd(r_v, τ_jkl) = 1. Here gcd(r_v, r_l) = 1, and gcd(r_v, τ_jkl) = 1 by (iv).
110: - Hence Q ≥ 5r_jr_kr_lτ_jkl·r_v = r_v[G:W].
111: - By the product formula, μ(U_vW) = Q/(5r_v·[G:W]) ≥ 1/5.
112: - U_vW ⊆ Z_jk ∩ Z_jl ∩ Z_kl, and each Z has measure 1/5. Hence Z_jk = Z_jl = Z_kl = U_vW.
113: 
114: **Step 3: contradiction.**
115: - Take any two pairs of vertices from V∖{v}. Two pairs that share a vertex lie in a common triple, and two disjoint pairs {j,k}, {l,m} are linked through {j,l}. So all ten sets Z coincide; call the common set Z, with μ(Z) = 1/5.
116: - For j ≠ k, X_j∩X_k = Z. So the five sets X_j∖Z are pairwise disjoint, each of measure 1/5.
117: - Hence μ(∪_j X_j) = μ(Z) + 5·(1/5) = 6/5 > 1, a contradiction. ∎
118: 
119: The markers E3 (when v ≠ E3) and E5 play no role in this proof. If E2 is a neighbour j, nothing changes either: only r_v is involved in the granularity, and only gcd(r_v, ·) enters Step 2.
120: 
121: ## 4. rules-k6-v3 L12 (RPLP)
122: 
123: **Setting.** Fix v and translate so that g_v = 1. Write N for the five vertices other than v. For j ∈ N put X_j = U_vU_j, Y_j = G∖X_j and D_j = U_vg_jU_j.
124: 
125: **Facts, by rules-k6-v1 L7 (R1–R3, proved in sun-coset5 proof-v4 §5, which uses only pairwise disjointness and holds verbatim for six cosets):**
126: - D_j ⊆ Y_j.
127: - μ(D_j) = q_j/5 with an integer q_j satisfying 1 ≤ q_j ≤ 5 − α_vj.
128: - d_j = q_jr_j.
129: 
130: Each admissible vector q ∈ ∏_j {1, …, 5 − α_vj} is treated separately. The true configuration has exactly one such q, and it is not known which. So the argument must hold for every q.
131: 
132: **Types.**
133: - Each of X_j, Y_j and D_j is left U_v-invariant, hence a union of right U_v-cosets.
134: - For a right coset C, its type is (A_C, S_C), where A_C = {j : C ⊆ Y_j} and S_C = {j : C ⊆ D_j}. Since D_j ⊆ Y_j, S_C ⊆ A_C. By L9(b), S_C is hereditary-allowed whenever |S_C| ≥ 2.
135: - For each type (A, S) with A ≠ ∅, let x(A,S) be the total measure of right cosets of that type, so x(A,S) ≥ 0.
136: - The cosets with A_C = ∅ are exactly those in ∩_j X_j, and U_v is one of them.
137: 
138: **Forced containments.** Suppose τ_min(v,w,j) ≥ α_vwα_wj.
139: - (T2) at apex w gives τ ≤ α_vwα_wj, so τ = α_vwα_wj.
140: - (T3) at apex v gives μ(U_v(U_w∩U_j)) = τ/(5α_wj) = α_vw/5 = μ(X_w).
141: - Hence X_w = U_v(U_w∩U_j) ⊆ X_j, that is, Y_j ⊆ Y_w.
142: - So every type with j ∈ A and w ∉ A has x = 0. The engine omits those columns, which is valid.
143: 
144: **Constraints satisfied by x.**
145: - **(E1_j)** Σ_{(A,S): j∈S} x(A,S) = μ(D_j) = q_j/5.
146: - **(E2_j)** Σ_{(A,S): j∈A} x(A,S) = μ(Y_j) = (5 − α_vj)/5.
147: - **(B_jk)** ylo_jk ≤ Σ_{(A,S): j,k∈A} x(A,S) = μ(Y_j∩Y_k) ≤ yhi_jk.
148:   - μ(Y_j∩Y_k) = 1 − μ(X_j) − μ(X_k) + μ(X_j∩X_k).
149:   - lo_jk ≤ μ(X_j∩X_k) ≤ hi_jk, by rules-k6-v1 L4. Those bounds hold for every admissible τ (M1) and every instance (M2).
150: - **(TOT)** Σ x(A,S) = μ(∪_j Y_j) ≤ 1 − μ(U_v) < 1.
151: 
152: **The LP and its dual.** Let OPT(q) be the minimum of Σ x over all x ≥ 0 on allowed columns that satisfy E1, E2 and B. The true x is feasible, so OPT(q) ≤ μ(∪_j Y_j) < 1. If the system is infeasible, there is a contradiction straight away.
153: 
154: Let a_j, b_j be free rationals and u_jk, l_jk ≥ 0. Suppose that for every allowed column (A,S),
155: - Σ_{j∈S} a_j + Σ_{j∈A} b_j − Σ_{j<k, j,k∈A} u_jk + Σ_{j<k, j,k∈A} l_jk ≤ 1. ……(★)
156: 
157: **Weak duality.** For any feasible x:
158: - Σ x ≥ Σ_{(A,S)} x(A,S)·[Σ_{j∈S} a_j + Σ_{j∈A} b_j − Σ_{j,k∈A} u_jk + Σ_{j,k∈A} l_jk], using (★) and x ≥ 0.
159: - Regrouping the right side by constraint gives Σ_j a_j·(q_j/5) + Σ_j b_j·(5 − α_vj)/5 − Σ u_jk·μ(Y_j∩Y_k) + Σ l_jk·μ(Y_j∩Y_k).
160: - Since u_jk ≥ 0 and l_jk ≥ 0, this is ≥ Σ_j a_jq_j/5 + Σ_j b_j(5 − α_vj)/5 − Σ u_jk·yhi_jk + Σ l_jk·ylo_jk =: Obj.
161: 
162: So if Obj ≥ 1, then Σ x ≥ 1 for every feasible x, contradicting (TOT).
163: 
164: **Farkas case.** If the primal system has no solution, a dual direction exists with (★) holding with right-hand side 0 and positive objective. Scaling it so that Obj = 1 satisfies (★) with right-hand side 0 ≤ 1. So the same check (★) together with Obj ≥ 1 certifies this case as well.
165: 
166: **Exactness.** All entries are rationals: q_j/5, (5 − α_vj)/5, lo, hi, and the dual entries. The certificate is checked in exact rational arithmetic.
167: 
168: **Conclusion.** If, for **every** admissible q at v, there is either
169: - an RLP certificate (rules-k6-v1 L7 dual y with value ≥ 1), or
170: - a vector (a, b, u, l) satisfying (★) on all allowed columns with Obj ≥ 1,
171: 
172: then vertex v admits no configuration, so the pattern is impossible. ∎
173: 
174: The hereditary-allowed test is applied with maxgcd. That is exact, or at least safe, as shown in §1.

SOURCE 04-rules-v1.md SHA256 3b135feae84ef8f5257aa5395e3b7207b20788134696a8aab7497fe44c9ab187 ORIGINAL /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/sun-coset6/rules-k6-v1.md
1: # rules-k6-v1: lemmas for the Core5(6) pattern engine
2: 
3: The host-clock freeze record is in `evidence/freeze-v1.sha256`. Corrections go only into new versions.
4: 
5: **Reading conventions.**
6: - "[NEW]" means only "written in this lane"; it is not a novelty claim.
7: - **[GEN]** marks lemmas proved for arbitrary indices n_i in sun-coset5 `rules-v1.md` (cb86c168…390f), together with its cumulative erratum `rules-v1-erratum-v3.md`. Their proofs do not depend on the index form. They are restated below with explicit hypotheses and specialised to Core5(6).
8: 
9: ## 0. Setting
10: 
11: **Harmonic configuration.** G is finite. U_1, …, U_k ≤ G have indices n_i = [G:U_i]. The cosets g_1U_1, …, g_kU_k are **pairwise disjoint**.
12: 
13: **Pairwise data.** For i ≠ j:
14: - g_ij = gcd(n_i, n_j);
15: - I_ij = [G:U_i∩U_j];
16: - α_ij = I_ij / lcm(n_i, n_j).
17: 
18: **Core5(6).** k = 6 and n_i = 5r_i, where the r_i are pairwise coprime. Then:
19: - g_ij = 5, lcm(n_i, n_j) = 5r_ir_j and I_ij = 5r_ir_jα_ij.
20: - **Special vertices:**
21:   - E2 is the vertex with r even, if any. Its class is c2 = 1 if v₂(r_E2) = 1, and c2 = 2 if v₂(r_E2) ≥ 2.
22:   - E3 is the vertex with 3 | r, if any.
23:   - E5 is the vertex with 5 | r, if any.
24:   - Any two of these may coincide.
25: 
26: **Inputs from the minimal counterexample frame** (README §2):
27: - Sun(k) for k ≤ 4 holds in every group, by Zhu Theorem 1.1 and L0.6.
28: - Sun(5) holds in every group, by sun-coset5 proof-v4. This is a stated dependence.
29: - Sun(6) holds in every proper subgroup of the minimal G.
30: 
31: ## L0. Basic facts [GEN; rules-v1 L0]
32: 
33: - **L0.1** Product formula: |XY| = |X||Y| / |X∩Y|.
34: - **L0.2** gU ∩ hV = ∅ if and only if g⁻¹h ∉ UV.
35: - **L0.3** |U_iU_j| = α_ij|G| / g_ij and 1 ≤ α_ij ≤ g_ij − 1. In Core5(6): α_ij ∈ {1, 2, 3, 4} and |U_iU_j| = α_ij|G|/5.
36:   - *Proof.* L0.1 gives |U_iU_j| = |G| I_ij / (n_i n_j) = α_ij|G| / g_ij. Disjointness and L0.2 give U_iU_j ≠ G.
37: - **L0.4** Every double coset AxB has size a multiple of |G| / gcd([G:A], [G:B]).
38: - **L0.5** AB ∩ AC is a union of (A, B∩C)-double cosets.
39: - **L0.6** If [G:A] and [G:B] are coprime, then AB = G, so every left coset of A meets every left coset of B. This is Sun's statement at k = 2.
40: 
41: ## L1. Triangle lemma T [GEN; rules-v1 L1]
42: 
43: For distinct a, b, c put L = lcm(n_a, n_b, n_c) and T = [G : U_a∩U_b∩U_c]. Then L | T, and τ = T/L satisfies:
44: 
45: - **(T1)** I_xy | Lτ for every pair {x, y}.
46: - **(T3)** |U_y(U_x∩U_z)| = |G| L τ / (n_y I_xz).
47: - **(T2)** |U_y(U_x∩U_z)| ≤ min(|U_yU_x|, |U_yU_z|).
48: 
49: **Core5(6) form.**
50: - L = 5r_ar_br_c, because the r's are pairwise coprime.
51: - (T1) is equivalent to α_xy | r_zτ.
52: - (T3) reads |U_y(U_x∩U_z)| = τ|G| / (5α_xz).
53: - (T2) is equivalent to τ ≤ α_xyα_xz and τ ≤ α_yzα_xz.
54: - So τ_max is the smallest, over the three vertices, of the product of the two α's at that vertex.
55: - τ_min is the lcm over the three edges of α_e / gcd(α_e, r_opp), where r_opp is the r of the vertex opposite the edge. The table below lists gcd(α, r_z) by E2/E3 status alone; a prime p ≥ 5 never divides α ≤ 4.
56: 
57: | α | gcd(α, r_z) |
58: |---|---|
59: | 1 | 1 |
60: | 2 | 2 if z = E2, else 1 |
61: | 3 | 3 if z = E3, else 1 |
62: | 4 | 4 if z = E2 with c2 = 2; 2 if z = E2 with c2 = 1; else 1 |
63: 
64: The admissible τ are exactly the multiples of τ_min that are ≤ τ_max.
65: 
66: **T-kill.** If τ_min > τ_max, no configuration has these data. T-kills are **triangle-local**: whether a triangle is forbidden depends only on its three α's and on the E2-class and E3 status of its three vertices.
67: 
68: ## L2. Links [GEN; rules-v1 L2]
69: 
70: If |U_y(U_x∩U_z)| = |U_yU_x|, then U_yU_x ⊆ U_yU_z and U_xU_y ⊆ U_zU_y.
71: 
72: In Core5(6) the hypothesis holds exactly when τ = α_xyα_xz.
73: 
74: ## L3. Lemma K [GEN; rules-v1 L3]
75: 
76: **Statement.** Let v be a vertex and S a set of other vertices such that, for every pair {i, j} ⊆ S, U_vU_i ⊆ U_jU_i or U_vU_j ⊆ U_iU_j. Then Σ_{i∈S} |U_iU_v| ≤ |G| − |U_v|. In Core5(6) this reads Σ_{i∈S} α_iv ≤ 4.
77: 
78: **Proof.**
79: 1. The sets g_iU_iU_v (i ∈ S) are pairwise disjoint, by L0.2.
80: 2. Each of them is right U_v-invariant and misses g_vU_v.
81: 3. Adding their sizes gives the bound.
82: 
83: **K1.** The pair condition at v holds for {i, j} when |U_i(U_v∩U_j)| = |U_iU_v| (L2 with apex i), or when |U_j(U_v∩U_i)| = |U_jU_v|. In Core5(6) this is τ_ivj = α_ivα_vj.
84: 
85: **K2.** If U_vU_j ⊆ U_vU_k, then α_vj + α_kj ≤ 4. This is L3 applied at j with S = {v, k}, using the inverse U_jU_v ⊆ U_kU_v.
86: 
87: ## L4. Vertex count P [GEN; rules-v1 L4]
88: 
89: Fix a vertex v. For each other vertex j put X_j = U_vU_j and Y_j = G ∖ X_j.
90: 
91: - **(P1)** |X_j| = α_vj/5, and U_v ⊆ X_j.
92: - **(P2)** |X_j ∩ X_k| is a multiple of u = 1/gcd(n_v, I_jk). In Core5(6), u = 1/(5·gcd(r_v, α_jk)), with the gcd read from the table in L1.
93: - **(P3)** |X_j ∩ X_k| ≥ max(τ_vjk/(5α_jk), |X_j| + |X_k| − 1).
94: - **(P4)** If X_j ⊆ X_k, then K2 holds.
95: - **(P5)** For every set S of other vertices: Σ_{j∈S} |Y_j| − Σ_{j<k in S} |Y_j ∩ Y_k| < 1, with |Y_j ∩ Y_k| = 1 − |X_j| − |X_k| + |X_j ∩ X_k|.
96: 
97: **How the engine applies P.** All sizes are in units of |G|.
98: - hi_jk is the largest multiple of u that is ≤ min(|X_j|, |X_k|). It is lowered by one unit u if equality would force a containment that K2 forbids. If |X_j| = |X_k|, equality forces equality of the two sets, so both K2 conditions must hold.
99: - lo_jk is the bound in (P3), rounded up to a multiple of u.
100: - **P-pair kill:** lo_jk > hi_jk.
101: - **PB kill:** (P5) fails when every intersection is set to its hi value.
102: 
103: ## L5. Fibre lemma F [GEN; rules-v1 L6], with k = 6 inputs
104: 
105: Let H ≤ G with [G:H] = m. For each l put t_l = [U_l : U_l∩H] and λ_l = [H : U_l∩H] = n_l t_l / m.
106: 
107: - **(F1)** g_lU_l meets exactly t_l left cosets of H, and 1 ≤ t_l ≤ m. Also t_l = 1 if and only if U_l ⊆ H.
108: - **(F2)** Fix a left coset C of H. The vertices whose cosets meet C give pairwise disjoint left cosets in H of the subgroups U_l∩H. So if s ≥ 2 of them meet C, some pair has gcd(λ_l, λ_l') ≥ s. The input used depends on s:
109:   - s ≤ 4: Zhu's theorem, or L0.6 when s = 2;
110:   - s = 5: Sun(5), a stated dependence;
111:   - s = 6: minimality, since H is proper.
112: - **(F3)** If U_i ⊆ H, then |U_iU_l| ≤ |HU_l| = t_l|G|/m. In Core5(6) with m = 5, this reads α_il ≤ t_l.
113: 
114: Proofs as in rules-v1 L6.
115: 
116: ## L6. H5: index-5 subgroups from α = 1 edges [NEW]
117: 
118: **Statement.** In Core5(6), suppose α_ab = 1 and V := {v ∉ {a, b} : α_va = α_vb} ≠ ∅. Then:
119: - H := U_aU_b is a subgroup of index 5;
120: - U_a ⊆ H and U_b ⊆ H;
121: - for every v ∈ V, t_v = [U_v : U_v∩H] = α_va. In particular U_v ⊆ H when α_va = 1.
122: 
123: This covers M-S Lemma 3.7 with a = 5, which is the case α_va = 1.
124: 
125: **Proof.** Fix v ∈ V and put c = α_va = α_vb ∈ {1, 2, 3, 4}. Work in the triangle {v, a, b}.
126: 1. **The bound τ ≤ c.** The vertex products are c at a, c at b and c² at v, so (T2) gives τ ≤ c.
127: 2. **c divides τ.** Suppose c ≥ 2. By (T1), c | r_b τ and c | r_a τ, using the edges va and vb.
128:    - If c is prime (2 or 3): r_a and r_b are coprime, so c fails to divide at least one of them. Hence c | τ.
129:    - If c = 4: one of r_a, r_b is odd, and that one forces 4 | τ.
130:    - So τ = c. For c = 1 the bound already gives τ = 1.
131: 3. **U_vU_a = U_vU_b.** By (T3) with apex v, |U_v(U_a∩U_b)| = τ/(5α_ab) = c/5 = |U_vU_a| = |U_vU_b|. Since U_v(U_a∩U_b) lies inside both, U_vU_a = U_v(U_a∩U_b) = U_vU_b. Call this set X.
132: 4. **X = U_vW for W = ⟨U_a, U_b⟩.** X is right-invariant under U_a and under U_b, hence under W. So U_vW ⊆ X ⊆ U_vW.
133: 5. **W has index 5.** |X| = c/5 < 1, so W ≠ G. Also [G:W] divides gcd(5r_a, 5r_b) = 5, since U_a ≤ W. Hence [G:W] = 5.
134: 6. **W = U_aU_b.** U_aU_b ⊆ W and |U_aU_b| = 1/5 = |W|.
135: 7. **t_v = c.** |U_vW| = t_v|W| = t_v/5, and |U_vW| = |X| = c/5.
136: 
137: W does not depend on v. ∎
138: 
139: ## L7. Lemma R in LP form [NEW]
140: 
141: **Setting.** A harmonic configuration (pairwise disjoint cosets). Fix a vertex v and translate so that g_v = 1. For j ≠ v put D_j = U_vg_jU_j and d_j = |D_j|/|U_j|.
142: 
143: **The fibre facts (R1)–(R5′).** These are proved in sun-coset5 proof-v4 §5; the proofs use only disjointness and L0.
144: - **(R1)** D_j ∩ U_vU_j = ∅.
145: - **(R2)** |D_j| is a positive multiple of |G|/gcd(n_v, n_j).
146: - **(R3)** Every right coset U_vy ⊆ D_j meets g_jU_j, in a right coset of a subgroup of U_v of index d_j.
147: - **(R5′)** If D_j ∩ D_k ≠ ∅, then gcd(d_j, d_k) ≥ 2.
148: 
149: **A strengthening (R4′).** Let C be a right U_v-coset and S_C = {j : C ⊆ D_j}. If |S_C| = s ≥ 2, then:
150: - every pair in S_C has gcd ≥ 2;
151: - some pair in S_C has gcd ≥ s.
152: 
153: *Proof of (R4′).* The pieces in C, right-translated into U_v and then inverted, are pairwise disjoint left cosets in U_v. Apply Sun(s) in U_v (s ≤ 5, using the inputs of §0) to all of them. For the "every pair" part, apply L0.6 to each pair.
154: 
155: **Core5(6) form.**
156: - |D_j| = q_j|G|/5 with 1 ≤ q_j ≤ 5 − α_vj, so d_j = q_jr_j.
157: - The common primes of d_j and d_k lie in {2, 3}: q ≤ 4, and no two r's share a prime. In particular E5 is irrelevant here.
158: 
159: **LP statement.**
160: - **Measure.** For each non-empty S, let μ(S) be the total size of the right U_v-cosets C with S_C = S.
161: - **Coverage.** Σ_{S∋j} μ(S) = |D_j| = q_j/5.
162: - **Total.** Σ_S μ(S) ≤ 1 − |U_v| < 1. Here U_v·1 lies in no D_j, by (R1).
163: - **Support.** μ(S) > 0 only for **allowed** S: either |S| = 1, or every pair in S has maxgcd ≥ 2 and some pair has maxgcd ≥ |S|. "maxgcd" is the largest value of gcd(q_jr_j, q_kr_k) over all r with the given special statuses.
164: - **Lower bound.** Let LPmin(q) be the minimum of Σ_S μ(S) over μ ≥ 0 on allowed S with Σ_{S∋j} μ(S) ≥ q_j/5. This relaxes the true coverage equalities, so LPmin(q) is at most the true total.
165: 
166: **RLP kill.** If LPmin(q) ≥ 1 for every admissible vector q, i.e. every q with 1 ≤ q_j ≤ 5 − α_vj, then vertex v is impossible.
167: 
168: **Evaluation of LPmin.** By LP duality,
169:   LPmin = max Σ_j (q_j/5) y_j subject to Σ_{j∈S} y_j ≤ 1 for every maximal allowed S, and y ≥ 0.
170: - Maximal sets suffice: every non-maximal allowed set lies inside an allowed superset, and y ≥ 0 makes its constraint redundant.
171: - The engine solves this packing LP by exact rational simplex with Bland's rule.
172: 
173: **Soundness of maxgcd.** Using maxgcd only enlarges the allowed family, which lowers LPmin. Hence a kill computed with maxgcd is a kill for every instance.
174: 
175: ## L8. Evaluation points, representatives and E5 [NEW]
176: 
177: **(M1) τ_min suffices.** Every admissible τ is a multiple of τ_min. The rules T, K, P and RLP are upward closed in τ:
178: - a K1 link at τ_min forces τ = τ_min;
179: - the (P3) lower bounds increase with τ;
180: - RLP and F do not read τ.
181: 
182: So a kill at τ_min is a kill for every admissible τ.
183: 
184: **(M2) Representatives suffice.** A pattern quantifies over all pairwise coprime r with given statuses: the E2 vertex and its class, the E3 vertex, and the E5 vertex.
185: - **How r enters each rule.** T, K, P and the support of RLP depend on r only through these statuses. For E2 the class matters for α = 4, and for maxgcd the 2-adic part of q_jr_j is v₂(q_j) + v₂(r_j). In the RLP maxgcd, E2 of class 2 contributes an unbounded v₂.
186: - **F rules.** gcd(λ_l, λ_l') involves only the primes 2, 3 and 5:
187:   - t ≤ 5 and m = 5, and the r's are coprime;
188:   - a class-1 E2 contributes v₂ = 1 exactly;
189:   - class-2 E2, E3 and E5 contribute exponents larger than any t-part. The representative exponents 2⁴, 3⁴ and 5⁴ dominate.
190:   - Because the kill condition is monotone in the gcds, a kill at the representative is a kill for every instance.
191: 
192: **E5.** E5 affects only the F rules: no α and no q is divisible by 5, and only one r is divisible by 5. **Every F certificate must refute every E5 placement, including "no E5".** The engine checks all seven placements.
193: 
194: ## Certificates and the order of rules
195: 
196: **Enumeration.**
197: - There are 8 special-placement types up to S6.
198: - For each type, backtracking runs over the 15 edges, with α ∈ {1, 2, 3, 4}.
199: - A partial labelling is pruned as soon as a completed triangle is T-forbidden. Its certificate is the triangle-local record: the vertices, the three α's and the statuses.
200: - Every complete T-consistent labelling (a "leaf") is tested in this order: K, RLP, P (P-pair, then PB), FH5.
201: 
202: **FH5.**
203: - For each 1-edge (a, b) with V ≠ ∅, use H5 with m = 5.
204: - Vertices fixed inside H: a, b and every v ∈ V with α_va = 1.
205: - Forced values: t_v = α_va for the other v ∈ V.
206: - Free vertices: t_l ranges over 1..5. Every met-coset set M_l with |M_l| = t_l is enumerated, and a's coset is fixed to label 0.
207: - An assignment is rejected iff (F3) fails at a vertex with t = 1, or (F2) fails in some coset.
208: - The certificate requires every assignment to be rejected for **every** E5 placement.
209: 
210: **Output.**
211: - Each leaf yields one certificate or the label SURVIVE.
212: - Leaves are deduplicated into orbits by a canonical form under the stabiliser of the special placement.
213: - **Controls** record observations only and do not gate the run. They are:
214:   - **Core(5) and Core_3(4) reruns.** The same enumerator and the same code paths for T, K, P and RLP are run with the constants changed to (d = 4, k = 5) for Core(5) and (d = 3, k = 4) for Core_3(4). FH5 is omitted, because H5 is specific to the prime index 5.
215:     - Core(5): the canonical orbit count must be 21,150. Every orbit these rules leave must be one of the orbits that sun-coset5 engine-v1 killed with N2 or FH2, or the orbit S-D. Any other orbit would mean a rule is unsound.
216:     - Core_3(4) (= M-S Prop 4.3): the expectation is that every orbit is killed.
217:   - Realisable configurations survive.
218:   - Selected mutations break their certificates.

SOURCE 05-rules-v2.md SHA256 b45049a09e93953465c61b72ead9dd7c137cb7e7fc997170c004694418b07fa6 ORIGINAL /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/sun-coset6/rules-k6-v2.md
1: # rules-k6-v2: the hereditary Sun condition (addendum to rules-k6-v1)
2: 
3: **Freeze and status.**
4: - The host-clock freeze record is in `evidence/freeze-v2.sha256`.
5: - `rules-k6-v1.md` (3b135fea…b187) stays frozen and unchanged.
6: - "[NEW]" means only "written in this lane"; it is not a novelty claim.
7: 
8: ## L9. Hereditary Sun condition [NEW]
9: 
10: **Statement.** Take the setting of rules-k6-v1 L5 (F2) or L7 (R4′). Let C be a left coset of H in (F2), or a right coset of U_v in (R4′). Let S_C be the set of vertices whose pieces lie in C, with local indices λ_l in (F2) or d_l = q_lr_l in (R4′). Then for **every** subset T ⊆ S_C with |T| ≥ 2, some pair in T has gcd ≥ |T|.
11: 
12: **Proof.**
13: 1. **The pieces of T are pairwise disjoint.** The pieces of all of S_C are pairwise disjoint, so in particular the pieces indexed by T are.
14: 2. **Move them into the subgroup.**
15:    - In (F2): left-translate by z⁻¹, where z ∈ C. This gives pairwise disjoint left cosets in H of the subgroups U_l∩H.
16:    - In (R4′): right-translate by w⁻¹, where w ∈ C, and then invert. This gives pairwise disjoint left cosets in U_v of subgroups of index d_l.
17: 
18:    The translation steps are those of rules-k6-v1 L5 and of sun-coset5 proof-v4 §5 (R5′).
19: 3. **Apply Sun(|T|).** Apply Sun's statement for k = |T| inside H, or inside U_v, using the inputs of rules-k6-v1 §0:
20:    - |T| = 2: L0.6.
21:    - |T| ∈ {3, 4}: Zhu Theorem 1.1.
22:    - |T| = 5: Sun(5). This rests on sun-coset5 proof-v4, a stated dependence.
23:    - |T| = 6: this can occur only in (F2), because in (R4′) S_C contains at most 5 vertices. Here use the minimality of G, since H is proper.
24: 
25:    So some pair of T has gcd ≥ |T|. ∎
26: 
27: **Effect on the rules.**
28: 
29: | Rule | Change |
30: |---|---|
31: | (F2) in FH5 | An assignment is rejected if the condition fails for **some** subset T of the vertices meeting a coset. rules-k6-v1 tested only T = S_C. |
32: | RLP (rules-k6-v1 L7) | A support S is **allowed** only if every subset T ⊆ S with \|T\| ≥ 2 has a pair with maxgcd ≥ \|T\|. |
33: 
34: The allowed family is therefore closed under taking subsets. The "maximal sets suffice" step and the soundness argument for maxgcd go through unchanged. A smaller allowed family can only raise LPmin, and the new condition is valid for every instance, so every RLP kill remains sound.
35: 
36: **Engine.** `code/core56_engine_v2.py` is `core56_engine_v1.py` with exactly two functions changed:
37: - `lp_min`: its allowed-support test becomes the hereditary condition;
38: - `fibre5_feasible`: its coset test becomes the hereditary condition.
39: 
40: The exact diff is in `evidence/engine-v1-to-v2.diff`. Everything else is unchanged: the enumeration, the T pruning, the canonical forms, K, P, M1/M2 and the E5 loop.

SOURCE 06-rules-v3.md SHA256 e25af84f71b7ebb6acf7f661c1a90d0c6c99cadc8510bc8575f39a926fc87130 ORIGINAL /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/sun-coset6/rules-k6-v3.md
1: # rules-k6-v3: lemmas XE, N2-6 and RPLP, plus the certificate formats (addendum to rules-k6-v1 and v2)
2: 
3: The host-clock freeze record is in `evidence/freeze-v3.sha256`. rules-k6-v1 (3b135fea…b187) and rules-k6-v2 (b45049a0…fa6) stay frozen. Here [NEW] means only "written in this lane". All sizes below are in units of |G|.
4: 
5: **Why these lemmas.** The frozen run engine-v2-core56 left 263 orbits (11:20 PDT). All of them have E2 at vertex 0 and E3 at vertex 1, and almost every α in them is 2 or 4. The lemmas below target that family.
6: 
7: ## L10. XE: equal products force an index-5 subgroup [NEW]
8: 
9: **Statement.** In Core5(6), let v, j, k be distinct and suppose U_vU_j = U_vU_k. Then α_jk = 1, W = U_jU_k is a subgroup of index 5, and [U_v : U_v∩W] = α_vj.
10: 
11: **Proof.**
12: 1. X := U_vU_j = U_vU_k is right-invariant under U_j and under U_k, hence under W := ⟨U_j, U_k⟩.
13: 2. Since U_v ⊆ X, we get U_vW ⊆ X. Also X = U_vU_j ⊆ U_vW. So X = U_vW.
14: 3. |X| = α_vj/5 < 1 (L0.3), so W ≠ G.
15: 4. Both U_j and U_k lie in W, so [G:W] divides gcd(5r_j, 5r_k) = 5. Hence [G:W] = 5.
16: 5. U_jU_k ⊆ W, so α_jk/5 = |U_jU_k| ≤ |W| = 1/5. Hence α_jk = 1, and then U_jU_k = W.
17: 6. Finally |U_vW| = [U_v : U_v∩W]·|W|, so α_vj/5 = [U_v : U_v∩W]/5. ∎
18: 
19: **Corollary.** If α_jk ≥ 2, then X_j ≠ X_k at every vertex v.
20: 
21: ## L11. N2-6 [NEW]
22: 
23: **Statement.** In Core5(6), let v be a vertex such that:
24: 1. r_v is odd, i.e. v ≠ E2;
25: 2. α_vj = 2 for all five j ≠ v;
26: 3. α_jk ∈ {2, 4} for every pair j, k ≠ v;
27: 4. if v = E3, then 3 ∤ τ_jkl for every triple j, k, l ≠ v. A sufficient check: in every such triangle, τ_max(jkl) < 3·τ_min(jkl) whenever 3 ∤ τ_min(jkl).
28: 
29: Then no configuration exists.
30: 
31: **Proof.** Translate so that g_v = 1. For j ≠ v put X_j = U_vU_j, so |X_j| = 2/5.
32: 
33: 1. **Each pairwise intersection is exactly a fifth.** Fix j ≠ k.
34:    - (T1) on the edge jk, whose opposite vertex is v: α_jk | r_vτ_vjk. Since r_v is odd and α_jk ∈ {2, 4}, α_jk | τ_vjk, so τ_vjk ≥ α_jk.
35:    - Put Z_jk := U_v(U_j∩U_k). By (T3), |Z_jk| = τ_vjk/(5α_jk) ≥ 1/5, and Z_jk ⊆ X_j∩X_k.
36:    - By (P2), |X_j∩X_k| is a multiple of 1/(5·gcd(r_v, α_jk)) = 1/5, because r_v is odd.
37:    - By XE, X_j ≠ X_k, so |X_j∩X_k| < 2/5.
38:    - Hence |X_j∩X_k| = 1/5. Then |Z_jk| = 1/5 and X_j∩X_k = Z_jk.
39: 2. **The three fifths of a triple coincide.** Fix a triple j, k, l ≠ v and put W = U_j∩U_k∩U_l.
40:    - [G:W] = 5r_jr_kr_lτ_jkl.
41:    - Q := [G : U_v∩W] is a common multiple of [G:W] and of T_vjk = 5r_vr_jr_kτ_vjk. So Q is a multiple of 5r_jr_k·lcm(r_lτ_jkl, r_vτ_vjk).
42:    - gcd(r_v, r_lτ_jkl) = gcd(r_v, τ_jkl). This equals 1: τ involves only the primes 2 and 3, r_v is odd, and either v ≠ E3 or 3 ∤ τ_jkl by condition 4.
43:    - So r_v·r_lτ_jkl divides that lcm, and Q ≥ r_v[G:W].
44:    - Therefore |U_vW| = Q/(5r_v[G:W]) ≥ 1/5.
45:    - U_vW ⊆ Z_jk ∩ Z_jl ∩ Z_kl, and each Z has size 1/5. Hence Z_jk = Z_jl = Z_kl.
46: 3. **Contradiction.** Any two pairs from the five neighbours are linked by a chain of triples, so all ten sets Z_jk are one set Z with |Z| = 1/5. The five sets X_j ∖ Z are pairwise disjoint, each of size 1/5. So |∪_j X_j| = 1/5 + 5/5 = 6/5 > 1. ∎
47: 
48: ## L12. RPLP: Lemma R combined with the vertex counts [NEW]
49: 
50: **Setting.** A harmonic configuration in Core5(6). Fix a vertex v and normalise g_v = 1. Write N for the five other vertices. For j ∈ N put:
51: - X_j = U_vU_j and Y_j = G ∖ X_j;
52: - D_j = U_vg_jU_j, with |D_j| = q_j/5, where 1 ≤ q_j ≤ 5 − α_vj.
53: 
54: **Which sets are right U_v-cosets unions.**
55: - X_j is left U_v-invariant, so X_j and Y_j are unions of right U_v-cosets.
56: - D_j is also left U_v-invariant, so it too is a union of right U_v-cosets.
57: - By (R1), D_j ⊆ Y_j.
58: 
59: **Types of right cosets.** Give each right U_v-coset C the type (A_C, S_C), where A_C = {j : C ⊆ Y_j} and S_C = {j : C ⊆ D_j}. Then S_C ⊆ A_C, and S_C is hereditary-allowed (rules-k6-v2 L9 with rules-k6-v1 L7, evaluated with maxgcd).
60: 
61: **Forced containments.** Suppose τ_min(v,w,j) ≥ α_vwα_wj. By (T2) this forces τ = α_vwα_wj. Then (T3) gives |U_v(U_w∩U_j)| = |X_w|, so X_w ⊆ X_j, that is, Y_j ⊆ Y_w. Hence a type with j ∈ A and w ∉ A has measure 0.
62: 
63: **The LP.** For every allowed type (A, S) with A ≠ ∅ that is consistent with the forced containments, let x(A,S) be the total size of right cosets of that type. Then:
64: - **(E1_j)** Σ_{S∋j} x(A,S) = q_j/5.
65: - **(E2_j)** Σ_{A∋j} x(A,S) = |Y_j| = (5 − α_vj)/5.
66: - **(U_jk), (L_jk)** ylo_jk ≤ Σ_{A⊇{j,k}} x(A,S) ≤ yhi_jk, where yhi/ylo = 1 − (α_vj + α_vk)/5 + hi_jk/lo_jk, with hi and lo as in the P rule (rules-k6-v1 L4).
67: - **(TOT)** Σ x(A,S) = |∪_j Y_j| ≤ 1 − |U_v| < 1, since U_v lies in no Y_j.
68: 
69: **RPLP kill.** Fix v. If, for every admissible q, the minimum of Σ x subject to E1, E2, U and L is ≥ 1 (or the system is infeasible), then vertex v is impossible.
70: 
71: **Certificate (weak duality).** Give a dual vector (a_j, b_j free; u_jk ≥ 0; l_jk ≥ 0) such that:
72: - for every allowed column (A, S): Σ_{j∈S} a_j + Σ_{j∈A} b_j − Σ_{{j,k}⊆A} u_jk + Σ_{{j,k}⊆A} l_jk ≤ 1;
73: - the dual objective satisfies Σ_j a_j q_j/5 + Σ_j b_j(5 − α_vj)/5 − Σ u_jk yhi_jk + Σ l_jk ylo_jk ≥ 1.
74: 
75: Weak duality then gives min Σ x ≥ 1. If the primal is infeasible, such a vector exists by Farkas' lemma.
76: 
77: **Soundness.**
78: - maxgcd only enlarges the set of allowed columns, which lowers the minimum.
79: - The lo and hi bounds are valid for every admissible τ (rules-k6-v1 L8, M1).
80: - A forced containment holds whenever its hypothesis holds at τ_min, because that hypothesis pins τ.
81: 
82: ## Certificate formats (engine v3; every one is checkable without trusting the engine)
83: 
84: 1. **T.** For each special type, the enumeration tree is deterministic. A pruned node records its triangle, its three α's and the statuses. The checker regenerates the tree independently. The engine publishes, for each type: the number of labelled leaves, the number of pruned nodes, and the sha256 of the sorted list of labelled leaf codes.
85: 2. **Orbits.** One line per canonical orbit: type, a representative α-matrix (as a code string), the rule, and its witness.
86: 3. **K (v, S), P (v, pair or S), N2-6 (v).** The checker recomputes each.
87: 4. **RLP (v).** For every admissible q at v, the global file `rlp_duals.json` holds an exact dual y ≥ 0 under the key "q|stats". The dual must satisfy Σ_{j∈S} y_j ≤ 1 for **every** allowed S (all of them, not only the maximal ones) and Σ (q_j/5) y_j ≥ 1.
88: 5. **FH5 ((a,b), V).** The checker re-runs the exhaustive fibre search for all seven E5 placements. The engine records the number of assignments examined in each placement.
89: 6. **RPLP (v).** For every admissible q, either an RLP dual (when the RLP LP for that q is already ≥ 1) or an RPLP dual vector (a, b, u, l) in a canonical constraint order: j in N ascending; then pairs (j,k) in lexicographic order. The checker rebuilds the columns, bounds and forced containments itself and verifies the vector exactly.

SOURCE 07-reduction-README.md SHA256 10fc493e5bf79f98fb805dc9e4b48becbf51fc7d7648848fa8874542dda5e2bf ORIGINAL /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/sun-coset6/README.md
1: # sun-coset6: Sun's disjoint-coset conjecture at k = 6 in every group
2: 
3: - **Slot:** math-1, transition 43.
4: - **Contract:** `institute/runs/six-lane-2026-10-16/screening/math-1-sun-coset6-contract.json` (sha256 17fe5a9e…1769, frozen 10:24:35 PDT).
5: - **Freeze time and hashes:** host-clock record in `evidence/freeze-v1.sha256`. Times written inside this file are not authoritative.
6: - **Rules (lemmas and proofs):** `rules-k6-v1.md`, frozen together with this file.
7: - **Labels:** "[NEW]" means only that something was written in this lane; it is not a novelty claim. Exploratory cells are labelled EXPLORATORY and are never counted.
8: 
9: ## 1. Exact target
10: 
11: **Statement.** Let a_1G_1, …, a_6G_6 be pairwise disjoint left cosets of finite-index subgroups of an arbitrary group G. The subgroups may coincide. Then gcd([G:G_i], [G:G_j]) ≥ 6 for some i < j. Otherwise an exact counterexample is required.
12: 
13: **Source and sharpness.** This is Sun, Internat. J. Math. 17 (2006), Conjecture 1.2, at k = 6. Six cosets of one subgroup of index 6 show the bound is sharp.
14: 
15: **Status.**
16: - The parent's pre-check (S/math-1-sun-coset6-priority-precheck-v1.md) located no k ≥ 6 claim for groups.
17: - Sun (MathOverflow Q316709, 2018) wrote: "since 2010, there is no further progress".
18: - For G = Z, O'Bryant proved k ≤ 20. That proof is integer-only and is not imported here.
19: - A search miss is not novelty.
20: 
21: ## 2. Reductions
22: 
23: **R0. Reduction to finite groups.** Same proof as sun-coset5 proof-v4 §1. core_G(G_i) is the kernel of the action of G on G/G_i, so it has index at most [G:G_i]!. The intersection of these cores is a normal subgroup of finite index lying inside every G_i. Every coset a_iG_i is the full preimage of its image in the quotient, so indices, intersections and disjointness all pass to the finite quotient.
24: 
25: **R1(6). Sun(6) ⟺ Core5(6), given the results for k ≤ 5.** [NEW; the parent re-derived it by hand; not yet hostile-reviewed]
26: 
27: > **Core5(6).** No finite group G has subgroups U_1, …, U_6 with [G:U_i] = 5r_i, the r_i pairwise coprime, and pairwise disjoint cosets g_iU_i.
28: 
29: **(Sun(6) ⇒ Core5(6)).** A Core5(6) configuration has every pairwise gcd equal to 5, and 5 < 6.
30: 
31: **(Core5(6) ⇒ Sun(6)), group by group.** Assume six disjoint cosets in G have pairwise gcds all ≤ 5, and write n_i = [G:G_i].
32: 
33: 1. **Each gcd is 2, 3, 4 or 5.** No gcd is 1, by the k = 2 case (L0.2 + L0.6). So each pair shares exactly one prime from {2, 3, 5} and no other prime.
34: 2. **The cover by A_p.** Put A_p = {i : p | n_i} for p ∈ {2, 3, 5}. Each of the 15 pairs lies in exactly one A_p. Hence Σ_p C(|A_p|, 2) = 15, and |A_p ∩ A_q| ≤ 1 for p ≠ q.
35:    - Suppose every |A_p| ≤ 5. The only values of C(|A_p|, 2) are 0, 1, 3, 6 and 10, and the only way for three of them to sum to 15 is 6 + 6 + 3, i.e. sizes {4, 4, 3}.
36:    - But two 4-sets meeting in at most one point have a union of at least 7 points, and there are only 6. So this case is impossible.
37:    - Therefore one prime p divides all six n_i.
38: 3. **p = 3.** All six gcds equal 3, which is < 4. Any four of the cosets contradict Zhu Theorem 1.1 at k = 4.
39: 4. **p = 2.** Write n_i = 2^{e_i}m_i with m_i odd. The m_i are pairwise coprime, min(e_i, e_j) ≤ 2, and at most one e_i ≥ 3.
40:    - If two indices have e = 1, then with any third index all pairwise gcds are 2 < 3. This contradicts Zhu Theorem 1.1 at k = 3.
41:    - If exactly one has e = 1, that index together with three others has the form (2r_1, 4r_2, 4r_3, 4r_4) with r_1 odd. This is excluded by Prop 4.5 as proved in sun-coset5 proof-v4 §2.
42:    - Otherwise any five of the indices form a Core(5) configuration in G. Core(5) is excluded by sun-coset5 proof-v4.
43: 5. **p = 5.** Every gcd is exactly 5. So n_i = 5r_i with the r_i pairwise coprime, which is a Core5(6) configuration in G.
44: 
45: **Dependence.** Branch p = 2 relies on Core(5) (sun-coset5 proof-v4; parent bridge PASS). That result still awaits the parent's correctness verdict. Any k = 6 gate is conditional on that verdict, as the contract states. k = 5 itself carries a prior unpublished claim (Z. Gong, as reported by Sun). That claim does not affect whether k = 6 is open.
46: 
47: **Minimal-counterexample frame.** Suppose Sun(6) fails. Choose G of minimal order among finite groups that contain six pairwise disjoint cosets with all pairwise index-gcds ≤ 5. R1(6) holds group by group, so G carries a Core5(6) configuration. In this frame:
48: - every proper subgroup of G satisfies Sun(6);
49: - every group satisfies Sun(k) for k ≤ 4 (Zhu Theorem 1.1, plus L0.6);
50: - every group satisfies Sun(5) (sun-coset5 proof-v4, under the stated dependence).
51: 
52: ## 3. The attack
53: 
54: **Patterns.** For a Core5(6) configuration put α_ij = [G:U_i∩U_j]/(5r_ir_j) ∈ {1, 2, 3, 4}. Special vertices:
55: - E2: the vertex with r even, together with its class (v₂(r) = 1, or v₂(r) ≥ 2);
56: - E3: the vertex with 3 | r;
57: - E5: the vertex with 5 | r. E5 matters only inside fibre rules (rules-k6-v1 L8).
58: 
59: **Enumeration.**
60: - Under S6, every (α, E2-class, E3) is equivalent to one with E2 and E3 in a canonical position. There are 8 types: none; E3 only; E2 of class 1 or 2 alone; E2 together with E3 on the same vertex; E2 and E3 on different vertices.
61: - For each type, all 4^15 α-labellings are enumerated by backtracking. Triangles are pruned with the T lemma, so every labelling that contains a T-forbidden triangle is covered by a triangle-local certificate.
62: - Every leaf, i.e. every T-consistent labelling, receives a rule certificate or is listed as a survivor. Leaves are deduplicated up to the stabiliser of the special placement.
63: 
64: **Order of work.**
65: 1. Frozen program.
66: 2. Second checker, coded independently.
67: 3. Written lemmas against the survivors, frozen in rules-k6-v2 and later.
68: 
69: **PARTIAL if needed.** If no survivor beyond the frozen list is eliminated by 15:30 PDT, the lane reports PARTIAL exactly as the contract defines it.
70: 
71: ## 4. Exploratory calibration before the freeze (EXPLORATORY; not counted; not evidence)
72: 
73: Eval-kernel cells run between 10:27 and 11:00 PDT tried T, K, P, a fibre rule and a Lemma-R LP rule on some special types.
74: 
75: | special type | T/K leaves | survivors |
76: |---|---|---|
77: | none | 15,513 | 0 |
78: | E3 only | 15,785 | 0 |
79: | E2, class 1 | 22,863 | 700 labelled (11 orbits) |
80: | E2, class 2 | 25,103 | 830 labelled (14 orbits) |
81: | other four types | not completed (cell hit its 1800 s timeout) | — |
82: 
83: In both E2 types, every surviving orbit had the E2 vertex with star (1,1,2,2,2) and all other α in {2, 4}.
84: 
85: No file was written by these cells, and they are not counted.

73: 
74: class CertError(Exception):
75:     """A certificate (or input) failed a check.  Always fail closed."""
76: 
77: 
78: class Timeout(Exception):
79:     pass
80: 
81: 
82: def need(cond, msg):
83:     if not cond:
84:         raise CertError(msg)
85: 
86: 
87: def is_int(x):
88:     return type(x) is int          # excludes bool
89: 
90: 
91: def tkey(typ):
92:     return str(tuple(typ))         # e.g. "(None, 0, None)", "(0, 2, 1)"
93: 
94: 
95: def bits(m):
96:     return [x for x in range(5) if (m >> x) & 1]
97: 
98: 
99: def popcount(m):
100:     return bin(m).count("1")
101: 
102: 
103: def submasks(m):
104:     """All submasks of m, from m down to 0 (inclusive)."""
105:     s = m
106:     while True:
107:         yield s
108:         if s == 0:
109:             return
110:         s = (s - 1) & m
111: 
112: 
113: def vp(n, p):
114:     need(is_int(n) and n >= 1, "vp of non-positive")
115:     c = 0
116:     while n % p == 0:
117:         n //= p
118:         c += 1
119:     return c
120: 
121: 
122: def lcm(a, b):
123:     return a * b // gcd(a, b)
124: 
125: 
126: def sha256_bytes(b):
127:     return hashlib.sha256(b).hexdigest()
128: 
129: 
130: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- markers / gcd table
131: def is_e2(z, typ):
132:     return typ[0] is not None and z == typ[0]
133: 
134: 
135: def is_e3(z, typ):
136:     return typ[2] is not None and z == typ[2]
137: 
138: 
139: def gtab(a, z, typ):
140:     """gcd(alpha, r_z) as in the CERT-FORMAT section 2 table (rules-k6-v1 L1)."""
141:     if a == 1:
142:         return 1
143:     if a == 2:
144:         return 2 if is_e2(z, typ) else 1
145:     if a == 3:
146:         return 3 if is_e3(z, typ) else 1
147:     if a == 4:
148:         if is_e2(z, typ):
149:             return 4 if typ[1] == 2 else 2
150:         return 1
151:     raise CertError("alpha out of range: %r" % (a,))
152: 
153: 
154: def parse_type(t):
155:     need(isinstance(t, list) and len(t) == 3, "type field malformed: %r" % (t,))
156:     e2, c2, e3 = t
157:     need(e2 is None or is_int(e2), "type e2 malformed: %r" % (t,))
158:     need(is_int(c2), "type c2 malformed: %r" % (t,))
159:     need(e3 is None or is_int(e3), "type e3 malformed: %r" % (t,))
160:     typ = (e2, c2, e3)
161:     need(typ in TYPES, "unknown type %r" % (t,))
162:     return typ
163: 
164: 
165: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- codes
166: def parse_code(s):
167:     need(isinstance(s, str) and len(s) == 15 and all(ch in CODECH for ch in s), "bad code %r" % (s,))
168:     return s
169: 
170: 
171: def alpha_matrix(code):
172:     A = [[0] * NV for _ in range(NV)]
173:     for f, (i, j) in enumerate(EDGES):
174:         a = ord(code[f]) - 48
175:         A[i][j] = a
176:         A[j][i] = a
177:     return A
178: 
179: 
180: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- rule T
181: def tau_min(A, typ, i, j, k):
182:     """lcm over the three edges e of alpha_e / gcd(alpha_e, r_opposite)."""
183:     t = 1
184:     for (x, y, z) in ((j, k, i), (i, k, j), (i, j, k)):
185:         a = A[x][y]
186:         g = gtab(a, z, typ)
187:         need(a % g == 0, "internal: gcd table")
188:         t = lcm(t, a // g)
189:     return t
190: 
191: 
192: def tau_max(A, i, j, k):
193:     """minimum over the three vertices of the product of the two alphas at that vertex."""
194:     return min(A[i][j] * A[i][k], A[j][i] * A[j][k], A[k][i] * A[k][j])
195: 
196: 
197: def tri_pass(A, typ, i, j, k):
198:     return tau_min(A, typ, i, j, k) <= tau_max(A, i, j, k)
199: 
200: 
201: def t_consistent(A, typ):
202:     return all(tri_pass(A, typ, i, j, k) for (i, j, k) in TRIANGLES)
203: 
204: 
205: def tri_table(typ, i, j, k):
206:     """tab[d_ij][d_ik][d_jk] = T passes on triangle (i,j,k) with alpha = d+1."""
207:     tab = [[[False] * 4 for _ in range(4)] for _ in range(4)]
208:     A = [[0] * NV for _ in range(NV)]
209:     for d1 in range(4):
210:         for d2 in range(4):
211:             for d3 in range(4):
212:                 A[i][j] = A[j][i] = d1 + 1
213:                 A[i][k] = A[k][i] = d2 + 1
214:                 A[j][k] = A[k][j] = d3 + 1
215:                 tab[d1][d2][d3] = tri_pass(A, typ, i, j, k)
216:     return tab
217: 
218: 
219: def enumerate_leaves(typ):
220:     """Primary enumeration: all T-consistent labellings of one type, sorted code strings.
221:     Vertex by vertex (= edge order E).  For vertex c every star (alpha_0c..alpha_{c-1}c) in
222:     {1..4}^c is considered; triangle {a,b,c} (a<b<c) is tested once its last edge (b,c) is
223:     assigned, which is the only pruning."""
224:     partials = list(CODECH)
225:     levels = [len(partials)]
226:     for c in range(2, NV):
227:         stars = ["".join(s) for s in itertools.product(CODECH, repeat=c)]   # increasing order
228:         pairs = []
229:         for a in range(c):
230:             for b in range(a + 1, c):
231:                 tab = tri_table(typ, a, b, c)
232:                 mk = {}
233:                 for dab in range(4):
234:                     m = 0
235:                     for si, s in enumerate(stars):
236:                         if tab[dab][ord(s[a]) - 49][ord(s[b]) - 49]:
237:                             m |= 1 << si
238:                     mk[CODECH[dab]] = m
239:                 pairs.append((EIDX[(a, b)], mk))
240:         full = (1 << len(stars)) - 1
241:         new = []
242:         app = new.append
243:         for p in partials:
244:             m = full
245:             for e, mk in pairs:
246:                 m &= mk[p[e]]
247:                 if not m:
248:                     break
249:             while m:
250:                 low = m & -m
251:                 app(p + stars[low.bit_length() - 1])
252:                 m ^= low
253:         partials = new
254:         levels.append(len(partials))
255:     for x in range(1, len(partials)):
256:         need(partials[x - 1] < partials[x], "internal: enumeration not strictly increasing")
257:     return partials, levels
258: 
259: 
260: def enumerate_leaves_dfs(typ, deadline=None):
261:     """Second, structurally different enumeration: depth-first over the 15 edges in order E,
262:     each of 1..4 tried, each completed triangle tested with the scalar T function."""
263:     comp = [[] for _ in range(15)]
264:     for (a, b, c) in TRIANGLES:
265:         comp[EIDX[(b, c)]].append((a, b, c))
266:     A = [[0] * NV for _ in range(NV)]
267:     cur = [""] * 15
268:     out = []
269:     nodes = [0]
270: 
271:     def rec(f):
272:         if f == 15:
273:             out.append("".join(cur))
274:             return
275:         i, j = EDGES[f]
276:         for a in (1, 2, 3, 4):
277:             nodes[0] += 1
278:             if deadline is not None and nodes[0] % 200000 == 0 and time.time() > deadline:
279:                 raise Timeout()
280:             A[i][j] = A[j][i] = a
281:             cur[f] = CODECH[a - 1]
282:             ok = True
283:             for (x, y, z) in comp[f]:
284:                 if not tri_pass(A, typ, x, y, z):
285:                     ok = False
286:                     break
287:             if ok:
288:                 rec(f + 1)
289:         A[i][j] = A[j][i] = 0
290: 
291:     try:
292:         rec(0)
293:     except Timeout:
294:         return None, nodes[0]
295:     return out, nodes[0]
296: 
297: 
298: def spot_check(typ, leafset, leaves, seed, n_random, n_leaf_samples):
299:     """Membership in the leaf set agrees with the scalar T test on random labellings and on all
300:     one-edge changes of sampled leaves."""
301:     rng = random.Random(seed)
302:     bad = 0
303:     pos = 0
304:     checked = 0
305:     for _ in range(n_random):
306:         s = "".join(rng.choice(CODECH) for _ in range(15))
307:         tc = t_consistent(alpha_matrix(s), typ)
308:         pos += tc
309:         bad += tc != (s in leafset)
310:         checked += 1
311:     for _ in range(n_leaf_samples):
312:         base = leaves[rng.randrange(len(leaves))]
313:         for f in range(15):
314:             for ch in CODECH:
315:                 if ch == base[f]:
316:                     continue
317:                 s = base[:f] + ch + base[f + 1:]
318:                 tc = t_consistent(alpha_matrix(s), typ)
319:                 pos += tc
320:                 bad += tc != (s in leafset)
321:                 checked += 1
322:     return {"checked": checked, "t_consistent_among_checked": pos, "disagreements": bad}
323: 
324: 
325: def leaf_hash(sorted_codes):
326:     """sha256 of the sorted code strings joined by newline, plus a final newline."""
327:     h = hashlib.sha256()
328:     CH = 65536
329:     for s in range(0, len(sorted_codes), CH):
330:         h.update(("\n".join(sorted_codes[s:s + CH]) + "\n").encode("ascii"))
331:     return h.hexdigest()
332:

334: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- types / orbits
335: def stabiliser(typ):
336:     fixed = [x for x in (typ[0], typ[2]) if x is not None]
337:     return [p for p in itertools.permutations(range(NV)) if all(p[x] == x for x in fixed)]
338: 
339: 
340: def _perm_placement(p, pl):
341:     e2, c2, e3 = pl
342:     return (None if e2 is None else p[e2], c2, None if e3 is None else p[e3])
343: 
344: 
345: def check_types_cover():
346:     """Every E2(class)/E3 placement maps to exactly one of the 8 types; stabilisers are as stated."""
347:     perms = list(itertools.permutations(range(NV)))
348:     pls = []
349:     for e2 in [None] + list(range(NV)):
350:         for c2 in ([0] if e2 is None else [1, 2]):
351:             for e3 in [None] + list(range(NV)):
352:                 pls.append((e2, c2, e3))
353:     hits = {}
354:     for pl in pls:
355:         reach = set(_perm_placement(p, pl) for p in perms) & set(TYPES)
356:         need(len(reach) == 1, "placement %r reaches %d types" % (pl, len(reach)))
357:         t = reach.pop()
358:         hits[tkey(t)] = hits.get(tkey(t), 0) + 1
359:     sizes = {}
360:     for typ in TYPES:
361:         st = stabiliser(typ)
362:         need(set(st) == set(p for p in perms if _perm_placement(p, typ) == typ), "stabiliser mismatch")
363:         sset = set(st)
364:         for p in st:
365:             for r in st:
366:                 need(tuple(p[r[x]] for x in range(NV)) in sset, "stabiliser not closed")
367:         sizes[tkey(typ)] = len(st)
368:     return {"placements": len(pls), "placements_per_type": hits, "stabiliser_sizes": sizes,
369:             "each_placement_maps_to_exactly_one_type": True}
370: 
371: 
372: def edge_src(p):
373:     """(p.alpha)[f] = alpha[src[f]], where (p.alpha)_{p(i)p(j)} = alpha_{ij}."""
374:     pinv = [0] * NV
375:     for i, pi in enumerate(p):
376:         pinv[pi] = i
377:     return [EIDX[(pinv[x], pinv[y])] for (x, y) in EDGES]
378: 
379: 
380: def act(p, code):
381:     src = edge_src(p)
382:     return "".join(code[s] for s in src)
383: 
384: 
385: def orbit_getters(perms):
386:     return [itemgetter(*edge_src(p)) for p in perms]
387: 
388: 
389: def check_orbit_coverage(typ, leafset, reps, codes=None):
390:     """Own orbits of the reps under the stabiliser must be pairwise disjoint, union = leaf set.
391:     Also (informational) whether the engine 'code' field lies in the orbit of rep / is its min."""
392:     getters = orbit_getters(stabiliser(typ))
393:     union = set()
394:     total = 0
395:     code_in = 0
396:     code_min = 0
397:     for n, r in enumerate(reps):
398:         o = {"".join(g(r)) for g in getters}
399:         total += len(o)
400:         union |= o
401:         if codes is not None:
402:             code_in += codes[n] in o
403:             code_min += codes[n] == min(o)
404:     res = {"lines": len(reps), "sum_orbit_sizes": total, "union_size": len(union),
405:            "duplicate_elements": total - len(union), "disjoint": total == len(union),
406:            "union_equals_leaves": union == leafset,
407:            "missing": len(leafset - union), "extra": len(union - leafset),
408:            "union_sha256": leaf_hash(sorted(union))}
409:     if codes is not None:
410:         res["code_field_in_stabiliser_orbit_of_rep"] = code_in
411:         res["code_field_equals_my_orbit_min"] = code_min
412:     res["ok"] = bool(res["disjoint"] and res["union_equals_leaves"])
413:     return res
414: 
415: 
416: def code_in_s6_orbit_sample(reps, codes, limit):
417:     """Informational: for up to `limit` lines, is the engine 'code' in the full S6 orbit of rep?"""
418:     getters = orbit_getters(list(itertools.permutations(range(NV))))
419:     n = min(limit, len(reps))
420:     hit = 0
421:     for x in range(n):
422:         o = {"".join(g(reps[x])) for g in getters}
423:         hit += codes[x] in o
424:     return {"sampled": n, "code_in_S6_orbit": hit}

427: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- rationals / JSON
428: def parse_rat(s):
429:     need(isinstance(s, str) and RAT_RE.fullmatch(s) is not None, "bad rational %r" % (s,))
430:     try:
431:         return Fraction(s)
432:     except ZeroDivisionError:
433:         raise CertError("zero denominator %r" % (s,))
434: 
435: 
436: def _strict_pairs(pairs):
437:     d = {}
438:     for k, v in pairs:
439:         need(k not in d, "duplicate JSON key %r" % (k,))
440:         d[k] = v
441:     return d
442: 
443: 
444: def strict_loads(raw):
445:     """json.loads that rejects duplicate keys (fail closed)."""
446:     return json.loads(raw, object_pairs_hook=_strict_pairs)
