449: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- neighbours / q / supports
450: def nbrs(v):
451:     return [j for j in range(NV) if j != v]
452: 
453: 
454: def st_of(j, typ):
455:     return (typ[1] if is_e2(j, typ) else 0, 1 if is_e3(j, typ) else 0)
456: 
457: 
458: def rlp_key(q, sts):
459:     return ",".join(str(x) for x in q) + "|" + ";".join("%d%d" % s for s in sts)
460: 
461: 
462: def q_space(A, v):
463:     """Every q in prod_{j in N} {1..5-alpha_vj}, first neighbour slowest (itertools.product)."""
464:     return list(itertools.product(*[range(1, 6 - A[v][j]) for j in nbrs(v)]))
465: 
466: 
467: _ALLOWED = {}
468: 
469: 
470: def allowed_masks(q, sts):
471:     """All non-empty S (bitmask over positions 0..4 of N) that are allowed: |S| = 1, or every
472:     T subset of S with |T| >= 2 has a pair with maxgcd >= |T|.  All 31 subsets are tested."""
473:     ex = []
474:     for x in range(5):
475:         d, f = sts[x]
476:         need(d in (0, 1, 2) and f in (0, 1), "bad status")
477:         e2 = vp(q[x], 2) + (0 if d == 0 else (1 if d == 1 else SAT))
478:         e3 = vp(q[x], 3) + (SAT if f == 1 else 0)
479:         ex.append((e2, e3))
480:     ex = tuple(ex)
481:     if ex in _ALLOWED:
482:         return _ALLOWED[ex]
483: 
484:     def mg(x, y):
485:         return (2 ** min(ex[x][0], ex[y][0])) * (3 ** min(ex[x][1], ex[y][1]))
486: 
487:     res = []
488:     for S in range(1, 32):
489:         if popcount(S) == 1:
490:             res.append(S)
491:             continue
492:         ok = True
493:         for T in submasks(S):
494:             tm = bits(T)
495:             if len(tm) < 2:
496:                 continue
497:             if not any(mg(x, y) >= len(tm) for x, y in itertools.combinations(tm, 2)):
498:                 ok = False
499:                 break
500:         if ok:
501:             res.append(S)
502:     res = tuple(res)
503:     _ALLOWED[ex] = res
504:     return res
505: 
506: 
507: def check_rlp_dual(q, sts, entry, allowed=None):
508:     need(isinstance(entry, dict) and "y" in entry, "dual entry malformed")
509:     ys = entry["y"]
510:     need(isinstance(ys, list) and len(ys) == 5, "dual y must have 5 entries")
511:     y = [parse_rat(s) for s in ys]
512:     need(all(t >= 0 for t in y), "dual y has a negative entry")
513:     if allowed is None:
514:         allowed = allowed_masks(q, sts)
515:     for S in allowed:
516:         tot = sum((y[x] for x in bits(S)), Fraction(0))
517:         need(tot <= 1, "allowed S=%s has y-sum %s > 1" % (bits(S), tot))
518:     obj = sum((Fraction(q[x], 5) * y[x] for x in range(5)), Fraction(0))
519:     need(obj >= 1, "dual objective %s < 1" % (obj,))
520:     return obj
521: 
522: 
523: class RLPCache:
524:     """Caches RLP dual checks by their full input key "q|statuses" (the key fixes q and statuses)."""
525: 
526:     def __init__(self, duals):
527:         need(isinstance(duals, dict), "rlp_duals.json is not an object")
528:         self.duals = duals
529:         self.res = {}
530: 
531:     def check(self, q, sts):
532:         key = rlp_key(q, sts)
533:         if key not in self.res:
534:             if key not in self.duals:
535:                 self.res[key] = (False, "missing dual for key %s" % key)
536:             else:
537:                 try:
538:                     check_rlp_dual(q, sts, self.duals[key])
539:                     self.res[key] = (True, "")
540:                 except CertError as e:
541:                     self.res[key] = (False, "key %s: %s" % (key, e))
542:         return self.res[key]
543: 
544: 
545: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- rules
546: def _vertex(x):
547:     return is_int(x) and 0 <= x < NV
548: 
549: 
550: def _vertex_set(S, v, minlen):
551:     return (isinstance(S, list) and len(S) >= minlen and all(_vertex(i) and i != v for i in S)
552:             and len(set(S)) == len(S))
553: 
554: 
555: def check_K(A, typ, wit):
556:     need(isinstance(wit, list) and len(wit) == 2, "K witness shape")
557:     v, S = wit
558:     need(_vertex(v), "K: bad v")
559:     need(_vertex_set(S, v, 1), "K: bad S")
560:     need(sum(A[i][v] for i in S) >= 5, "K: sum of alpha_iv < 5")
561:     for i, j in itertools.combinations(S, 2):
562:         need(tau_min(A, typ, i, v, j) == A[i][v] * A[v][j], "K: pair (%d,%d) not linked at v=%d" % (i, j, v))
563:     return "ok"
564: 
565: 
566: def check_RLP(A, typ, wit, cache):
567:     need(isinstance(wit, list) and len(wit) == 1, "RLP witness shape")
568:     v = wit[0]
569:     need(_vertex(v), "RLP: bad v")
570:     sts = [st_of(j, typ) for j in nbrs(v)]
571:     nq = 0
572:     for q in q_space(A, v):
573:         ok, msg = cache.check(q, sts)
574:         need(ok, "RLP q=%s: %s" % (q, msg))
575:         nq += 1
576:     return "ok (%d q)" % nq
577: 
578: 
579: def lohi(A, typ, v, j, k):
580:     """lo/hi of |X_j cap X_k| at vertex v (units |G|), rounded on the grid of multiples of u:
581:     lo = u*ceil(L/u), hi = u*floor(min(x_j,x_k)/u), then the K2 containment decrement."""
582:     xj = Fraction(A[v][j], 5)
583:     xk = Fraction(A[v][k], 5)
584:     g = gtab(A[j][k], v, typ)
585:     u = Fraction(1, 5 * g)
586:     L = max(Fraction(tau_min(A, typ, v, j, k), 5 * A[j][k]), xj + xk - 1, Fraction(0))
587:     lo = u * math.ceil(L / u)
588:     m = min(xj, xk)
589:     hi = u * math.floor(m / u)
590:     if hi == m:
591:         allow_j_in_k = A[v][j] + A[k][j] <= 4     # K2 for X_j subset X_k
592:         allow_k_in_j = A[v][k] + A[j][k] <= 4     # K2 for X_k subset X_j
593:         if xj < xk:
594:             barred = not allow_j_in_k
595:         elif xk < xj:
596:             barred = not allow_k_in_j
597:         else:
598:             barred = not (allow_j_in_k and allow_k_in_j)
599:         if barred:
600:             hi = hi - u
601:     return lo, hi, u
602: 
603: 
604: def check_Ppair(A, typ, wit):
605:     need(isinstance(wit, list) and len(wit) == 2, "Ppair witness shape")
606:     v, pr = wit
607:     need(_vertex(v), "Ppair: bad v")
608:     need(_vertex_set(pr, v, 2) and len(pr) == 2, "Ppair: bad pair")
609:     lo, hi, u = lohi(A, typ, v, pr[0], pr[1])
610:     need(lo > hi, "Ppair: lo %s <= hi %s" % (lo, hi))
611:     return "ok"
612: 
613: 
614: def check_PB(A, typ, wit):
615:     need(isinstance(wit, list) and len(wit) == 2, "PB witness shape")
616:     v, S = wit
617:     need(_vertex(v), "PB: bad v")
618:     need(_vertex_set(S, v, 1), "PB: bad S")
619:     N = nbrs(v)
620:     H = {}
621:     for j, k in itertools.combinations(N, 2):
622:         lo, hi, u = lohi(A, typ, v, j, k)
623:         need(lo <= hi, "PB: pair (%d,%d) has lo > hi (Ppair applies instead)" % (j, k))
624:         H[(j, k)] = hi
625:         H[(k, j)] = hi
626:     x = {j: Fraction(A[v][j], 5) for j in N}
627:     lhs = sum((1 - x[j] for j in S), Fraction(0)) - sum(
628:         (1 - x[j] - x[k] + H[(j, k)] for j, k in itertools.combinations(S, 2)), Fraction(0))
629:     need(lhs >= 1, "PB: Bonferroni lhs %s < 1" % (lhs,))
630:     return "ok"
631: 
632: 
633: def check_N26(A, typ, wit):
634:     """Safe N2-6: v != E2; alpha_vj = 2; alpha_jk in {2,4}; for every neighbour triple and EVERY
635:     admissible tau (multiples of tau_min up to tau_max): no prime factor >= 5, and if v = E3 then
636:     3 does not divide tau; and (as instructed) tau_max < 3 tau_min on every triple when v = E3.
637:     The '3 | tau_min' alternative of the texts is never accepted."""
638:     need(isinstance(wit, list) and len(wit) == 1, "N26 witness shape")
639:     v = wit[0]
640:     need(_vertex(v), "N26: bad v")
641:     need(not is_e2(v, typ), "N26: v is E2")
642:     N = nbrs(v)
643:     need(all(A[v][j] == 2 for j in N), "N26: some alpha_vj != 2")
644:     need(all(A[j][k] in (2, 4) for j, k in itertools.combinations(N, 2)), "N26: some alpha_jk not in {2,4}")
645:     for (j, k, l) in itertools.combinations(N, 3):
646:         tm = tau_min(A, typ, j, k, l)
647:         tM = tau_max(A, j, k, l)
648:         need(tm <= tM, "N26: triple (%d,%d,%d) T-inconsistent" % (j, k, l))
649:         for tau in range(tm, tM + 1, tm):
650:             t = tau
651:             while t % 2 == 0:
652:                 t //= 2
653:             while t % 3 == 0:
654:                 t //= 3
655:             need(t == 1, "N26: admissible tau=%d on (%d,%d,%d) has a prime factor >= 5" % (tau, j, k, l))
656:             if is_e3(v, typ):
657:                 need(tau % 3 != 0, "N26: v=E3 and admissible tau=%d on (%d,%d,%d) divisible by 3" % (tau, j, k, l))
658:         if is_e3(v, typ):
659:             need(tM < 3 * tm, "N26: v=E3 and tau_max %d >= 3*tau_min %d on (%d,%d,%d)" % (tM, tm, j, k, l))
660:     return "ok"
661: 
662: 
663: def forced_pairs(A, typ, v):
664:     """Ordered position pairs (w,j) of N with tau_min(v,w,j) >= alpha_vw * alpha_wj."""
665:     N = nbrs(v)
666:     return [(wx, jx) for wx in range(5) for jx in range(5)
667:             if wx != jx and tau_min(A, typ, v, N[wx], N[jx]) >= A[v][N[wx]] * A[N[wx]][N[jx]]]
668: 
669: 
670: def rplp_columns(A, typ, v, q, sts, forced=None, include_empty_S=True):
671:     """All (A,S): A non-empty subset of N closed under forced pairs (j in A => w in A);
672:     S subset of A that is empty, a singleton or hereditary-allowed (for this q)."""
673:     if forced is None:
674:         forced = forced_pairs(A, typ, v)
675:     allowed = set(allowed_masks(q, sts))
676:     cols = []
677:     for Am in range(1, 32):
678:         if any(((Am >> jx) & 1) and not ((Am >> wx) & 1) for (wx, jx) in forced):
679:             continue
680:         for Sm in submasks(Am):
681:             if (Sm == 0 and include_empty_S) or (Sm != 0 and Sm in allowed):
682:                 cols.append((Am, Sm))
683:     return cols
684: 
685: 
686: def rplp_bounds(A, typ, v):
687:     N = nbrs(v)
688:     yhi, ylo = [], []
689:     for (x, y) in PAIRS5:
690:         lo, hi, u = lohi(A, typ, v, N[x], N[y])
691:         base = 1 - Fraction(A[v][N[x]] + A[v][N[y]], 5)
692:         yhi.append(base + hi)
693:         ylo.append(base + lo)
694:     return yhi, ylo
695: 
696: 
697: def check_rplp_vec(A, typ, v, q, vec, cols, yhi, ylo):
698:     need(isinstance(vec, list) and len(vec) == 30, "RPLP: P vector must have 30 entries")
699:     vals = [parse_rat(s) for s in vec]
700:     a, b, u, l = vals[0:5], vals[5:10], vals[10:20], vals[20:30]
701:     need(all(t >= 0 for t in u), "RPLP: negative u")
702:     need(all(t >= 0 for t in l), "RPLP: negative l")
703:     N = nbrs(v)
704:     for (Am, Sm) in cols:
705:         val = sum((a[x] for x in bits(Sm)), Fraction(0)) + sum((b[x] for x in bits(Am)), Fraction(0))
706:         for p, (x, y) in enumerate(PAIRS5):
707:             if (Am >> x) & 1 and (Am >> y) & 1:
708:                 val += l[p] - u[p]
709:         need(val <= 1, "RPLP: column A=%s S=%s has value %s > 1" % (bits(Am), bits(Sm), val))
710:     obj = (sum((a[x] * Fraction(q[x], 5) for x in range(5)), Fraction(0))
711:            + sum((b[x] * Fraction(5 - A[v][N[x]], 5) for x in range(5)), Fraction(0))
712:            - sum((u[p] * yhi[p] for p in range(10)), Fraction(0))
713:            + sum((l[p] * ylo[p] for p in range(10)), Fraction(0)))
714:     need(obj >= 1, "RPLP: objective %s < 1" % (obj,))
715:     return obj
716: 
717: 
718: def check_RPLP(A, typ, wit, cache):
719:     need(isinstance(wit, list) and len(wit) == 2, "RPLP witness shape")
720:     v, certs = wit
721:     need(_vertex(v), "RPLP: bad v")
722:     N = nbrs(v)
723:     sts = [st_of(j, typ) for j in N]
724:     qs = q_space(A, v)
725:     need(isinstance(certs, list) and len(certs) == len(qs),
726:          "RPLP: %s certs for %d admissible q" % (len(certs) if isinstance(certs, list) else "?", len(qs)))
727:     forced = forced_pairs(A, typ, v)
728:     yhi, ylo = rplp_bounds(A, typ, v)
729:     nR = nP = 0
730:     for q, cert in zip(qs, certs):
731:         need(isinstance(cert, list) and len(cert) == 2 and cert[0] in ("R", "P"), "RPLP: malformed cert for q=%s" % (q,))
732:         if cert[0] == "R":
733:             key = rlp_key(q, sts)
734:             need(cert[1] == key, "RPLP: R key %r != expected %r" % (cert[1], key))
735:             ok, msg = cache.check(q, sts)
736:             need(ok, "RPLP q=%s: %s" % (q, msg))
737:             nR += 1
738:         else:
739:             cols = rplp_columns(A, typ, v, q, sts, forced)
740:             try:
741:                 check_rplp_vec(A, typ, v, q, cert[1], cols, yhi, ylo)
742:             except CertError as e:
743:                 raise CertError("q=%s: %s" % (q, e))
744:             nP += 1
745:     return "ok (R %d, P %d)" % (nR, nP)
746: 
747: 
748: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- FH5
749: def fh5_V(A, a, b):
750:     return sorted(v for v in range(NV) if v != a and v != b and A[v][a] == A[v][b])
751: 
752: 
753: def check_FH5_witness(A, typ, wit):
754:     need(isinstance(wit, list) and len(wit) == 3, "FH5 witness shape")
755:     ab, V, counts = wit
756:     need(isinstance(ab, list) and len(ab) == 2 and all(_vertex(x) for x in ab) and ab[0] != ab[1], "FH5: bad [a,b]")
757:     a, b = ab
758:     need(A[a][b] == 1, "FH5: alpha_ab != 1")
759:     myV = fh5_V(A, a, b)
760:     need(len(myV) >= 1, "FH5: V empty")
761:     need(isinstance(V, list) and all(_vertex(x) for x in V) and len(set(V)) == len(V) and sorted(V) == myV,
762:          "FH5: witness V %r != computed V %r" % (V, myV))
763:     need(isinstance(counts, list) and len(counts) == 7 and all(is_int(c) and c >= 0 for c in counts),
764:          "FH5: counts malformed")
765:     return a, b, myV, counts
766: 
767: 
768: def fh5_domains(A, a, b, V):
769:     subsets = {t: [m for m in range(1, 32) if popcount(m) == t] for t in range(1, 6)}
770:     dom = {a: [(1, 1)]}
771:     dom[b] = [(1, 1 << c) for c in range(5)]
772:     for l in V:
773:         c = A[l][a]
774:         dom[l] = [(1, 1 << x) for x in range(5)] if c == 1 else [(c, m) for m in subsets[c]]
775:     for l in range(NV):
776:         if l not in dom:
777:             dom[l] = [(t, m) for t in range(1, 6) for m in subsets[t]]
778:     return dom
779: 
780: 
781: def fh5_lambda_exp(typ, l, t, e5):
782:     e2 = vp(t, 2) + ((1 if typ[1] == 1 else SAT) if is_e2(l, typ) else 0)
783:     e3 = vp(t, 3) + (SAT if is_e3(l, typ) else 0)
784:     e5x = vp(t, 5) + (SAT if (e5 is not None and l == e5) else 0)
785:     return e2, e3, e5x
786: 
787: 
788: def fh5_fgcd(typ, x, y, e5):
789:     e, f = fh5_lambda_exp(typ, x[0], x[1], e5), fh5_lambda_exp(typ, y[0], y[1], e5)
790:     return (2 ** min(e[0], f[0])) * (3 ** min(e[1], f[1])) * (5 ** min(e[2], f[2]))
791: 
792: 
793: def fh5_search(A, typ, a, b, V, deadline=None):
794:     """Exhaustive search over all FH5 assignments, all 7 E5 placements at once.
795:     A partial assignment is dropped for a placement only when it is already rejected there by
796:     (i) F3 between two assigned vertices or (ii) a failing subset T of an assigned coset member
797:     set; both persist under extension.  Status REFUTED / ACCEPTED / INCOMPLETE."""
798:     NP = len(E5_PLACEMENTS)
799:     FULL = (1 << NP) - 1
800:     others = [l for l in range(NV) if l != a and l != b and l not in V]
801:     order = [a, b] + list(V) + others
802:     dom = fh5_domains(A, a, b, V)
803:     space = 1
804:     for l in range(NV):
805:         space *= len(dom[l])
806:     fgc = {}
807: 
808:     def fg(x, y, pi):
809:         key = (x, y, pi)
810:         r = fgc.get(key)
811:         if r is None:
812:             r = fh5_fgcd(typ, x, y, E5_PLACEMENTS[pi])
813:             fgc[key] = r
814:         return r
815: 
816:     def coset_ok(M, new, pi):
817:         n = len(M)
818:         for tm in range(1, 1 << n):
819:             T = [M[i] for i in range(n) if (tm >> i) & 1]
820:             T.append(new)
821:             s = len(T)
822:             if not any(fg(T[i], T[j], pi) >= s for i in range(s) for j in range(i + 1, s)):
823:                 return False
824:         return True
825: 
826:     members = [[] for _ in range(5)]
827:     assigned = []
828:     stats = {"nodes": 0}
829:     found = []
830: 
831:     def dfs(depth, alive):
832:         stats["nodes"] += 1
833:         if deadline is not None and stats["nodes"] % 2048 == 0 and time.time() > deadline:
834:             raise Timeout()
835:         if depth == NV:
836:             found.append(([(l, t, bits(m)) for (l, t, m) in assigned],
837:                           [E5_PLACEMENTS[i] for i in range(NP) if (alive >> i) & 1]))
838:             return True
839:         l = order[depth]
840:         for (t, m) in dom[l]:
841:             bad = False
842:             for (i, ti, mi) in assigned:
843:                 if (ti == 1 and A[i][l] > t) or (t == 1 and A[l][i] > ti):
844:                     bad = True
845:                     break
846:             if bad:
847:                 continue
848:             na = alive
849:             for pi in range(NP):
850:                 if not (na >> pi) & 1:
851:                     continue
852:                 for c in range(5):
853:                     if (m >> c) & 1 and members[c] and not coset_ok(members[c], (l, t), pi):
854:                         na &= ~(1 << pi)
855:                         break
856:             if na == 0:
857:                 continue
858:             assigned.append((l, t, m))
859:             for c in range(5):
860:                 if (m >> c) & 1:
861:                     members[c].append((l, t))
862:             r = dfs(depth + 1, na)
863:             for c in range(5):
864:                 if (m >> c) & 1:
865:                     members[c].pop()
866:             assigned.pop()
867:             if r:
868:                 return True
869:         return False
870: 
871:     try:
872:         acc = dfs(0, FULL)
873:     except Timeout:
874:         return {"status": "INCOMPLETE", "nodes": stats["nodes"], "space": space}
875:     if acc:
876:         return {"status": "ACCEPTED", "nodes": stats["nodes"], "space": space, "accepted": found[0]}
877:     return {"status": "REFUTED", "nodes": stats["nodes"], "space": space}
878: 
879: 
880: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- input lines
881: def parse_line(raw):
882:     obj = strict_loads(raw)
883:     need(isinstance(obj, dict), "line is not an object")
884:     rule = obj.get("rule")
885:     need(isinstance(rule, str) and rule in RULES, "unknown rule %r" % (rule,))
886:     expected = {"type", "code", "rep", "rule"} | (set() if rule == "SURVIVE" else {"witness"})
887:     need(set(obj.keys()) == expected, "unexpected keys %r" % (sorted(obj.keys()),))
888:     typ = parse_type(obj["type"])
889:     rep = parse_code(obj["rep"])
890:     code = parse_code(obj["code"])
891:     return typ, rep, code, rule, obj.get("witness")
892: 
893: 
894: def check_rule(A, typ, rule, wit, cache, deadline=None):
895:     """Dispatch one line's certificate.  Raises CertError on failure.  FH5 runs the full search."""
896:     if rule == "SURVIVE":
897:         raise CertError("SURVIVE line (no certificate)")
898:     if rule == "K":
899:         return check_K(A, typ, wit), None
900:     if rule == "RLP":
901:         return check_RLP(A, typ, wit, cache), None
902:     if rule == "PB":
903:         return check_PB(A, typ, wit), None
904:     if rule == "Ppair":
905:         return check_Ppair(A, typ, wit), None
906:     if rule == "N26":
907:         return check_N26(A, typ, wit), None
908:     if rule == "RPLP":
909:         return check_RPLP(A, typ, wit, cache), None
910:     if rule == "FH5":
911:         a, b, V, counts = check_FH5_witness(A, typ, wit)
912:         r = fh5_search(A, typ, a, b, V, deadline)
913:         r["engine_counts_equal_full_space"] = all(c == r["space"] for c in counts)
914:         if r["status"] == "INCOMPLETE":
915:             return "INCOMPLETE", r
916:         need(r["status"] == "REFUTED", "FH5: accepted assignment %r" % (r.get("accepted"),))
917:         return "ok", r
918:     raise CertError("unknown rule %r" % (rule,))
919: 
920: 
921: def compare_type_summary(k, leaves_mine, hash_mine, orbit_lines, rule_counts_lines, eng):
922:     """Compare my numbers for one type with run.json summary[type]; returns failure strings."""
923:     out = []
924:     if not isinstance(eng, dict):
925:         return ["run.json has no summary for %s" % k]
926:     if eng.get("leaves_labelled") != leaves_mine:
927:         out.append("leaf count mismatch: mine %d, run.json %r" % (leaves_mine, eng.get("leaves_labelled")))
928:     if eng.get("leafcodes_sha256") != hash_mine:
929:         out.append("leaf hash mismatch: mine %s, run.json %r" % (hash_mine, eng.get("leafcodes_sha256")))
930:     if eng.get("orbits") != orbit_lines:
931:         out.append("orbit count mismatch: lines %d, run.json %r" % (orbit_lines, eng.get("orbits")))
932:     erc = eng.get("orbit_rule_counts")
933:     if not (isinstance(erc, dict) and {a: b for a, b in erc.items() if b} == rule_counts_lines):
934:         out.append("rule counts mismatch: lines %r, run.json %r" % (rule_counts_lines, erc))
935:     return out
936: 
937: 
938: def global_survive_check(rules_of_lines, survivors, run):
939:     """Any SURVIVE line, non-empty survivors.json or non-zero total_survivors => failure."""
940:     out = []
941:     ns = sum(1 for r in rules_of_lines if r == "SURVIVE")
942:     if ns:
943:         out.append("%d SURVIVE lines" % ns)
944:     if survivors not in ([], {}):
945:         out.append("survivors.json is not empty")
946:     if not isinstance(run, dict) or run.get("total_survivors") != 0:
947:         out.append("run.json total_survivors is not 0")
948:     return out

951: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- main
952: def main(argv=None):
953:     ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
954:     ap.add_argument("--engine-dir", required=True)
955:     ap.add_argument("--out-json", required=True)
956:     ap.add_argument("--out-md", required=True)
957:     ap.add_argument("--log", required=True)
958:     ap.add_argument("--budget-seconds", type=int, default=3300)
959:     ap.add_argument("--spot-random", type=int, default=20000)
960:     ap.add_argument("--spot-leaves", type=int, default=300)
961:     args = ap.parse_args(argv)
962:     tmpdir = os.environ.get("TMPDIR", "")
963:     need(tmpdir != "" and os.path.isdir(tmpdir), "TMPDIR must be set to an existing own directory")
964:     import tempfile
965:     tempfile.tempdir = tmpdir
966:     st0 = os.statvfs("/Users/jinleic/repos/aifours")
967:     free0 = st0.f_bavail * st0.f_frsize
968:     if free0 < FREE_DISK_FLOOR:
969:         print("refusing to start: free disk %d < %d bytes" % (free0, FREE_DISK_FLOOR))
970:         return 2
971:     t0 = time.time()
972:     deadline = t0 + args.budget_seconds
973:     logf = open(args.log, "x")
974: 
975:     def log(msg):
976:         line = "%s %8.1fs %s" % (time.strftime("%H:%M:%S"), time.time() - t0, msg)
977:         logf.write(line + "\n")
978:         logf.flush()
979:         print(line, flush=True)
980: 
981:     rep = {"checker": {}, "inputs": {}, "sources_used_sha256": {}, "environment": {},
982:            "types_cover": None, "per_type": {}, "global": {}, "failures": [], "incomplete": []}
983:     me = os.path.abspath(__file__)
984:     rep["checker"] = {"path": me, "sha256": sha256_bytes(open(me, "rb").read())}
985:     rep["environment"] = {"python": sys.version, "platform": platform.platform(), "TMPDIR": tmpdir,
986:                           "processes": 1, "numpy": "not used", "budget_seconds": args.budget_seconds,
987:                           "free_bytes_at_start": free0, "started": time.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S%z")}
988:     for p in SOURCES_USED:
989:         try:
990:             rep["sources_used_sha256"][p] = sha256_bytes(open(p, "rb").read())
991:         except OSError as e:
992:             rep["sources_used_sha256"][p] = "UNREADABLE: %s" % e
993: 
994:     def fail(where, msg):
995:         rep["failures"].append({"where": where, "msg": msg})
996: 
997:     # ---- inputs
998:     files = {}
999:     for name in ("run.json", "orbits.jsonl.gz", "rlp_duals.json", "survivors.json"):
1000:         p = os.path.join(args.engine_dir, name)
1001:         data = open(p, "rb").read()
1002:         files[name] = data
1003:         rep["inputs"][p] = {"sha256": sha256_bytes(data), "bytes": len(data)}
1004:     man = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(args.engine_dir)), "engine-v3-run.sha256")
1005:     if os.path.exists(man):
1006:         mb = open(man, "rb").read()
1007:         rep["inputs"][man] = {"sha256": sha256_bytes(mb), "bytes": len(mb)}
1008:         mtxt = mb.decode("utf-8", "replace")
1009:         rep["global"]["manifest_lists_input_hashes"] = {
1010:             name: rep["inputs"][os.path.join(args.engine_dir, name)]["sha256"] in mtxt for name in files}
1011: 
1012:     def load_json(name):
1013:         try:
1014:             return strict_loads(files[name])
1015:         except CertError as e:
1016:             fail(name, "strict JSON parse: %s" % e)
1017:             return json.loads(files[name])
1018: 
1019:     run = load_json("run.json")

1227: def render_md(rep):
1228:     g = rep["global"]
1229:     out = ["# Independent checker report (checker_v1, standard library, one process)\n"]
1230:     out.append("- Verdict: **%s** (failures %d, incomplete parts %d)" % (
1231:         rep["verdict"], rep["failures_total"], len(rep["incomplete"])))
1232:     out.append("- Checker: `%s` sha256 `%s`" % (rep["checker"]["path"], rep["checker"]["sha256"]))
1233:     out.append("- Elapsed: %s s (one process, no numpy)" % (rep["environment"]["elapsed_seconds"],))
1234:     out.append("\n## Inputs (sha256)\n")
1235:     for p, v in rep["inputs"].items():
1236:         out.append("- `%s` %s (%d bytes)" % (p, v["sha256"], v["bytes"]))
1237:     out.append("- orbits.jsonl (uncompressed) %s; equals run.json field: %s" % (
1238:         g.get("orbits_jsonl_uncompressed_sha256"), g.get("orbits_jsonl_uncompressed_sha256_matches_run_json")))
1239:     out.append("\n## Per type\n")
1240:     out.append("| type | leaves (mine) | leaves (run.json) | hash match | edge-DFS = primary | orbit lines | orbits (run.json) | coverage ok | verified | failed | incomplete |")
1241:     out.append("|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|")
1242:     for k, pt in rep["per_type"].items():
1243:         if "leaves_mine" not in pt:
1244:             out.append("| `%s` | INCOMPLETE | | | | | | | | | |" % k)
1245:             continue
1246:         dc = pt["dfs_crosscheck"]
1247:         dcs = dc["identical_to_primary"] if isinstance(dc, dict) else dc
1248:         rules = pt.get("rules", {})
1249:         ver = sum(r["verified"] for r in rules.values())
1250:         fl = sum(r["failed"] for r in rules.values())
1251:         inc = sum(r["incomplete"] for r in rules.values())
1252:         es = pt.get("engine_summary") or {}
1253:         out.append("| `%s` | %d | %s | %s | %s | %d | %s | %s | %d | %d | %d |" % (
1254:             k, pt["leaves_mine"], es.get("leaves_labelled"), pt["hash_match"], dcs, pt["orbit_lines"],
1255:             es.get("orbits"), pt["orbit_coverage"]["ok"], ver, fl, inc))
1256:     out.append("\nEngine `code` field (informational; CERT-FORMAT says not to rely on it): lines whose `code` lies in the "
1257:                "stabiliser orbit of `rep`, per type: %s; sampled lines whose `code` lies in the full S6 orbit of `rep`: %s." % (
1258:                    {k: pt.get("orbit_coverage", {}).get("code_field_in_stabiliser_orbit_of_rep") for k, pt in rep["per_type"].items()},

1264:         out.append("| %s | %d | %d | %d | %d |" % (r, v["lines"], v["verified"], v["failed"], v["incomplete"]))
1265:     out.append("\n## Global\n")
1266:     for key in ("lines_parsed", "lines_malformed", "survive_lines", "survivors_json_empty", "total_orbits_match",
1267:                 "rlp_dual_entries", "rlp_keys_checked", "rlp_keys_passed", "rlp_keys_failed",
1268:                 "rlp_dual_entries_unused", "leaves_total_mine", "manifest_lists_input_hashes"):
1269:         out.append("- %s: %s" % (key, g.get(key)))
1270:     out.append("- FH5: %s" % (g.get("fh5"),))
1271:     out.append("- q statistics: %s" % (g.get("q_statistics"),))
1272:     out.append("- types cover: %s" % (rep.get("types_cover"),))
1273:     if rep["failures"]:
1274:         out.append("\n## First failures\n")
1275:         for f in rep["failures"][:30]:
1276:             out.append("- %s: %s" % (f["where"], f["msg"]))
1277:     if rep["incomplete"]:
1278:         out.append("\n## Incomplete\n")
1279:         for f in rep["incomplete"][:30]:
1280:             out.append("- %s: %s" % (f["where"], f["what"]))
1281:     out.append(STATIC_NOTES)
1282:     out.append("")
1283:     return "\n".join(out)

1285: 
1286: STATIC_NOTES = """
1287: ## Method (what a PASS means)
1288: 
1289: - **Types.** A placement is (E2 vertex or none, class c2 in {1,2}, E3 vertex or none): 1 + 6 + 6*2 + 6*2*6 = 91
1290:   placements.  Coverage proof: no markers -> (None,0,None); only E3 at x -> any permutation with x->0 gives
1291:   (None,0,0); only E2 at x with class c -> x->0 gives (0,c,None); E2 and E3 both at x -> x->0 gives (0,c,0);
1292:   E2 at x, E3 at y != x -> x->0, y->1 gives (0,c,1).  Presence of each marker, the class and coincidence are
1293:   S6-invariant, so the image type is unique.  Relabelling the six subgroups by a permutation maps a configuration
1294:   to a configuration, so every pattern is equivalent to one with a type's placement.  The stabiliser of a type's
1295:   placement is exactly {p : p(e2)=e2 if present, p(e3)=e3 if present} (E2 and E3 are different markers and are never
1296:   exchanged); sizes 720, 120 (x5), 24 (x2).  `check_types_cover` re-verifies all of this by brute force over S6.
1297: - **Universe.** Primary enumeration: vertex-by-vertex stars, every star in {1..4}^c tried, a triangle tested exactly
1298:   when its last edge is assigned.  Cross-check: an edge-by-edge depth-first enumeration with the scalar T test
1299:   (must give the identical sorted list), plus random and one-edge-neighbour spot checks.  The sorted list is hashed as
1300:   specified and compared with run.json.
1301: - **Orbits.** For each line the checker computes the full stabiliser orbit of `rep` itself; the orbits must be pairwise
1302:   disjoint and their union must equal the leaf set exactly (set equality, not only counts/hash); `rep` must be a
1303:   T-consistent leaf of the line's type.  Every rule is checked on `rep`; a certificate for `rep` transfers to its orbit
1304:   because each rule is label-equivariant and the stabiliser fixes the markers.
1305: - **Rules.** K, RLP (all 31 non-empty supports with every hereditary sub-test, exact Fractions, every admissible q,
1306:   key recomputed), PB/Ppair (grid rounding lo = u*ceil(L/u), hi = u*floor(min/u) then the K2 decrement), FH5
1307:   (exhaustive search over the whole assignment space for all 7 E5 placements; pruning only on rejections that persist),
1308:   N26 (safe form: every admissible tau of every neighbour triple has no prime factor >= 5, and if v = E3 none is
1309:   divisible by 3 and tau_max < 3 tau_min; the '3 | tau_min' alternative is never accepted), RPLP (columns rebuilt:
1310:   every non-empty A closed under the ordered forced pairs, every S subset of A that is empty, a singleton or
1311:   hereditary-allowed; per-q certificate count exact; R keys recomputed).  Any SURVIVE line, malformed line, unknown
1312:   rule, duplicate JSON key, non-string rational or sign violation is a failure.
1313: 
1314: ## Observations on the texts (no effect on this verdict unless stated)
1315: 
1316: 1. CERT-FORMAT-k6-v1 N26 and rules-k6-v3 L11(4) allow '3 | tau_min' as an alternative; that direction is wrong
1317:    (it forces 3 | tau).  Unreachable here because tau_min is a power of 2 under alpha_jk in {2,4}; fixed in
1318:    CERT-FORMAT-k6-v2 / erratum v2 F1.  This checker uses the safe test.
1319: 2. CERT-FORMAT-k6-v1 PB wording ('ceil(...) rounded up to a multiple of u') is ambiguous; the integer-ceiling reading
1320:    would be unsound.  This checker uses grid rounding (as in CERT-FORMAT-k6-v2 / erratum v2 F3).
1321: 3. The engine `code` field is not, in general, a labelling in the stabiliser orbit of `rep` (counts above); it is in
1322:    the full S6 orbit on every sampled line, i.e. it appears to carry the markers elsewhere.  Coverage here is
1323:    established from `rep` only, as CERT-FORMAT section 3 prescribes.
1324: 4. The PB precondition 'every pair has lo <= hi' is not needed for soundness (it is enforced anyway).
1325: 5. E5 enters only FH5; for K, P, RLP and RPLP E5-independence is a written argument (alpha, q <= 4 and the gcd
1326:    table involve no factor 5).  For N26 this checker removes the dependence mechanically (prime-support test on
1327:    every admissible tau).
1328: 6. What a PASS supports (wording from the delta reviews): no minimal-order Sun(6) counterexample carries a Core5(6)
1329:    configuration, conditional on the written rule proofs, the imports (Zhu k <= 4, accepted Sun(5)), and R1(6).
1330:    This report is a certificate check only; it makes no theorem or novelty claim.
1331: """
1332: 
1333: 
1334: if __name__ == "__main__":
1335:     sys.exit(main())

25: def load_checker(path):
26:     spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("checker_under_test", path)
27:     m = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
28:     spec.loader.exec_module(m)
29:     return m

32: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- independent naive FH5 evaluator
33: def naive_fh5_counts(A, typ, a, b, V):
34:     """Full enumeration of the FH5 assignment space (no pruning), written separately from the
35:     checker directly from the CERT-FORMAT FH5 text.  Returns the number of NON-rejected
36:     assignments for each of the 7 E5 placements (None, 0..5).  SAT is 50 here (checker uses 30)."""
37:     SATX = 50
38: 
39:     def v_p(n, p):
40:         c = 0
41:         while n % p == 0:
42:             n //= p
43:             c += 1
44:         return c
45: 
46:     e2v, c2, e3v = typ
47: 
48:     def subsets(t):
49:         return [frozenset(c) for c in itertools.combinations(range(5), t)]
50: 
51:     doms = []
52:     for l in range(6):
53:         if l == a:
54:             doms.append([(1, frozenset([0]))])
55:         elif l == b or (l in V and A[l][a] == 1):
56:             doms.append([(1, frozenset([c])) for c in range(5)])
57:         elif l in V:
58:             doms.append([(A[l][a], s) for s in subsets(A[l][a])])
59:         else:
60:             doms.append([(t, s) for t in range(1, 6) for s in subsets(t)])
61: 
62:     def lam(l, t, e5):
63:         x2 = v_p(t, 2) + ((1 if c2 == 1 else SATX) if (e2v is not None and l == e2v) else 0)
64:         x3 = v_p(t, 3) + (SATX if (e3v is not None and l == e3v) else 0)
65:         x5 = v_p(t, 5) + (SATX if (e5 is not None and l == e5) else 0)
66:         return x2, x3, x5
67: 
68:     def fgcd(l, t, l2, t2, e5):
69:         p, q = lam(l, t, e5), lam(l2, t2, e5)
70:         return 2 ** min(p[0], q[0]) * 3 ** min(p[1], q[1]) * 5 ** min(p[2], q[2])
71: 
72:     counts = []
73:     for e5 in [None, 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5]:
74:         acc = 0
75:         for ch in itertools.product(*doms):
76:             rej = False
77:             for i in range(6):
78:                 if ch[i][0] == 1 and any(l != i and A[i][l] > ch[l][0] for l in range(6)):
79:                     rej = True
80:                     break
81:             if not rej:
82:                 for c in range(5):
83:                     M = [l for l in range(6) if c in ch[l][1]]
84:                     for r in range(2, len(M) + 1):
85:                         for T in itertools.combinations(M, r):
86:                             if all(fgcd(x, ch[x][0], y, ch[y][0], e5) < r for x, y in itertools.combinations(T, 2)):
87:                                 rej = True
88:                                 break
89:                         if rej:
90:                             break
91:                     if rej:
92:                         break
93:             acc += not rej
94:         counts.append(acc)
95:     return counts
96:

98: def main(argv=None):
99:     ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
100:     ap.add_argument("--checker", required=True)
101:     ap.add_argument("--engine-dir", required=True)
102:     ap.add_argument("--out", required=True)
103:     args = ap.parse_args(argv)
104:     t0 = time.time()
105:     C = load_checker(args.checker)
106:     results = []
107: 
108:     def record(name, kind, expected, observed, detail=""):
109:         results.append({"name": name, "kind": kind, "expected": expected, "observed": observed,
110:                         "pass": expected == observed, "detail": str(detail)[:400]})
111:         print("%-4s %-60s expected %-8s observed %-8s" % ("ok" if expected == observed else "FAIL", name, expected, observed), flush=True)
112: 
113:     def verdict(fn):
114:         try:
115:             fn()
116:             return "ACCEPT", ""
117:         except C.CertError as e:
118:             return "REJECT", str(e)
119: 
120:     inputs = {}
121:     raw = {}
122:     for name in ("run.json", "orbits.jsonl.gz", "rlp_duals.json", "survivors.json"):
123:         p = os.path.join(args.engine_dir, name)
124:         raw[name] = open(p, "rb").read()
125:         inputs[p] = hashlib.sha256(raw[name]).hexdigest()
126:     run = C.strict_loads(raw["run.json"])

Sun reports a prior Z.Gong confirmation dated2009/2010; its proof was not located or inspected. No novelty claim, publication, or human peer review is implied.

7: ## Statement
8: For **every group** G and five pairwise disjoint left cosets a_iG_i of subgroups with finite indices, some i<j satisfies gcd([G:G_i],[G:G_j])≥5. The subgroups may coincide; the cosets need not cover G. Infinite G is included by the proved finite-quotient reduction. No all-k theorem is claimed.

36: 
37: ## Theorem
38: 
39: Let G be any group. Let a_1G_1, …, a_5G_5 be pairwise disjoint left cosets of subgroups of finite index. Then gcd([G:G_i], [G:G_j]) ≥ 5 for some i < j.
40: 
41: ## 1. R0: reduction to finite groups
42: 
43: core_G(G_i) is the kernel of the action of G on G/G_i, so its index is at most [G:G_i]!. Hence N = ∩_i core_G(G_i) is a normal subgroup of finite index contained in every G_i.
44: 
45: Each coset a_iG_i is the full preimage of its image in G/N. So indices, intersection indices and disjointness all pass to the finite group G/N.
46: 
47: ## 2. R1: reduction to Core(5), and a proof of M-S Prop 4.5
48: 
49: **Core(5).** No finite group G has subgroups U_1, …, U_5 with [G:U_i] = 4r_i, the r_i pairwise coprime, and pairwise disjoint cosets g_iU_i.
50: 
51: **The Sun(5) ⇒ Core(5) direction.** A Core(5) configuration has every pairwise gcd equal to 4 < 5.
52: 
53: **The Core(5) ⇒ Sun(5) direction, group by group.** Let n_i = [G:G_i], and assume five disjoint cosets in G have all pairwise gcds ≤ 4.
54: 
55: 1. **Each gcd is 2, 3 or 4.** By L0.2 and L0.6 no gcd equals 1, so each lies in {2, 3, 4}.
56: 2. **All divisible by 3, or all even.** If some n_i is odd, every gcd with it is odd and so equals 3. Then 3 divides all five indices.
57: 3. **All divisible by 3.** Every gcd is then 3 < 4. Any four of the cosets contradict Zhu Theorem 1.1 at k = 4.
58: 4. **All even.** Write n_i = 2^{e_i}m_i with m_i odd. Then the m_i are pairwise coprime, every e_i ≥ 1, and at most one e_i ≥ 3.
59:    - **Two indices with e = 1.** With any third index they have all pairwise gcds equal to 2 < 3, contradicting Zhu Theorem 1.1 at k = 3.
60:    - **Exactly one index with e = 1.** We get (2r_1, 4r_2, 4r_3, 4r_4) with r_1 odd and the r's pairwise coprime. This is excluded by Proposition 4.5 below.
61:    - **Otherwise.** n_i = 4r_i with r_i = 2^{e_i−2}m_i pairwise coprime: a Core(5) configuration in the same group G, with the same subgroups.
62: 
63: **Proposition 4.5 (M-S, Beitr. Algebra Geom. 60 (2019); proof patched per F-2 and F-8).**
64: 
65: *Status of this proof.* It is self-contained modulo rules-v1 L0.3–L0.5 and L1–L3. "Harmonic" means: there exist g_i such that the cosets g_iU_i are pairwise disjoint (F-9).
66: 
67: *Statement.* Let r_1, …, r_4 be pairwise coprime with r_1 odd. Then no group has subgroups with indices [G:U_1] = 2r_1 and [G:U_i] = 4r_i (i = 2, 3, 4) and pairwise disjoint cosets g_iU_i.
68: 
69: *Proof.* By R0 we may take G finite. Sizes are in units of |G|, and α(i,j) := [G:U_i∩U_j] / lcm(n_i, n_j). Lemma K (rules-v1 L3) at a vertex v with set S requires: for each pair {i, j} ⊆ S, either U_vU_i ⊆ U_jU_i or U_vU_j ⊆ U_iU_j. Its conclusion is Σ_{i∈S} |U_iU_v| < 1.
70: 
71: - **(a) α(1,i) = 1 and |U_1U_i| = 1/2 for i ≥ 2.** gcd(2r_1, 4r_i) = 2, since r_1 is odd and coprime to r_i. So α ≤ 1 by L0.3.
72: - **(b) α(i,j) ≠ 2 for i, j ≥ 2.** Suppose α(2,3) = 2, so [G:U_2∩U_3] = 8r_2r_3.
73:   - T = [G:U_1∩U_2∩U_3] is divisible by 2r_1 and by 8r_2r_3, hence by 8r_1r_2r_3.
74:   - So |U_1(U_2∩U_3)| = T/(2r_1·8r_2r_3) ≥ 1/2 = |U_1U_2| = |U_1U_3|. Hence U_1U_2 = U_1(U_2∩U_3) = U_1U_3.
75:   - **Inverting** gives U_2U_1 = U_3U_1. In particular U_2U_1 ⊆ U_3U_1, which is L3's first alternative at v = 2 for the pair {1, 3}. This is K2 with (v, j, k) = (1, 2, 3).
76:   - Lemma K at v = 2 with S = {1, 3} then requires |U_1U_2| + |U_3U_2| < 1, but the sum is 1/2 + 1/2 = 1. Contradiction.
77: - **(c) If α(2,3) = 3, then 3 | r_1 and |U_2U_1 ∩ U_2U_3| = 1/4.** (F-2 patch.)
78:   - T = [G:U_1∩U_2∩U_3] is divisible by lcm(2r_1, 4r_2, 4r_3) = 4r_1r_2r_3. Write T = 4r_1r_2r_3τ.
79:   - [G:U_2∩U_3] = 12r_2r_3 divides T, so 3 | r_1τ.
80:   - [G:U_1∩U_3] = 4r_1r_3 by (a). So |U_2(U_1∩U_3)| = T/(4r_2·4r_1r_3) = τ/4 ≤ |U_2U_1| = 1/2. Hence τ ≤ 2, so 3 ∤ τ, and therefore 3 | r_1.
81:   - U_2U_1 ∩ U_2U_3 contains U_2(U_1∩U_3), so its size is ≥ τ/4 ≥ 1/4. By L0.5 and L0.4 it is a union of (U_2, U_1∩U_3)-double cosets, each of size a multiple of 1/gcd(4r_2, 4r_1r_3) = 1/4.
82:   - Its size is not 1/2. Otherwise U_2U_1 ⊆ U_2U_3. **Inverting** gives U_1U_2 ⊆ U_3U_2, which is L3's first alternative at v = 1 for the pair {2, 3} (K2 with (v, j, k) = (2, 1, 3)). Lemma K at v = 1 with S = {2, 3} would then give |U_2U_1| + |U_3U_1| = 1, a contradiction. Hence the size is exactly 1/4.
83: - **(d) Not all of α(2,3), α(2,4), α(3,4) equal 1.** Suppose they all do.
84:   - In the triangle {2, 3, 4}, (T2) gives τ ≤ 1, so τ = 1. Then |U_2(U_4∩U_3)| = 1/4 = |U_2U_4| (T3), so U_2U_4 ⊆ U_2U_3. **Inverting** gives U_4U_2 ⊆ U_3U_2. Likewise U_4U_3 ⊆ U_2U_3.
85:   - In the triangle {1, 2, 4}, T is a multiple of lcm(2r_1, 4r_2, 4r_4) = 4r_1r_2r_4. So |U_1(U_4∩U_2)| = T/(2r_1·4r_2r_4) ≥ 1/2 = |U_1U_4|, giving U_1U_4 ⊆ U_1U_2. **Inverting** gives U_4U_1 ⊆ U_2U_1. Likewise U_4U_1 ⊆ U_3U_1.
86:   - So for every pair {i, j} ⊆ S = {1, 2, 3}, the inclusion U_4U_i ⊆ U_jU_i holds for at least one ordering of the pair. That is L3's pair condition at v = 4.
87:   - Lemma K requires 1/2 + 1/4 + 1/4 < 1, which fails.
88: - **(e) Choosing labels.** By (b) and (d), together with α ≤ 3 from L0.3, some α(i,j) with i, j ≥ 2 equals 3. Relabel so that α(2,3) = 3. By (c), 3 | r_1.
89:   - α(2,4) and α(3,4) cannot both be 1. Otherwise the triangle {2, 3, 4} has τ ≤ α(2,4)α(3,4) = 1, and then 12r_2r_3 | T = 4r_2r_3r_4 gives 3 | r_4. That contradicts 3 | r_1.
90:   - By (b) neither of them equals 2. So, after swapping 2 and 3 if necessary, α(2,4) = 3.
91: - **(f) α(3,4) = 3.** By (b), α(3,4) ≠ 2. Suppose α(3,4) = 1.
92:   - In the triangle {2, 3, 4}, write T = 4r_2r_3r_4τ. Then 12r_2r_3 | T and 12r_2r_4 | T. Since 3 ∤ r_3r_4 (because 3 | r_1), we get 3 | τ.
93:   - So |U_3(U_2∩U_4)| = τ/(4·3) ≥ 1/4 = |U_3U_4|, giving U_3U_4 ⊆ U_3U_2. **Inverting** gives U_4U_3 ⊆ U_2U_3, which is L3's second alternative at v = 4 for the pair {2, 3} (K2 with (v, j, k) = (3, 4, 2)).
94:   - Lemma K at v = 4 with S = {2, 3} requires 3/4 + 1/4 < 1, which fails.
95: - **(g) Final count.** Now α(1,i) = 1 and α(i,j) = 3 for all i, j ∈ {2, 3, 4}, and 3 | r_1.
96:   - By (c), applied to the pairs (2,3) and (2,4): |U_2U_1 ∪ U_2U_j| = 1/2 + 3/4 − 1/4 = 1 for j = 3, 4.
97:   - |U_2U_3 ∩ U_2U_4| is a multiple of 1/gcd(4r_2, 12r_3r_4) = 1/4 (by L0.5 + L0.4, using 3 ∤ r_2). This is F-8.
98:   - That size is at least 3/4 + 3/4 − 1 = 1/2. It is not 3/4: otherwise U_2U_3 = U_2U_4, and K2 with (v, j, k) = (2, 3, 4) — that is, Lemma K at v = 3 with S = {2, 4}, using the inverse U_3U_2 ⊆ U_4U_2 — would give 3/4 + 3/4 < 1. So the size is 1/2, and U_2U_3 ∪ U_2U_4 = G.
99:   - Put 𝒜 = U_2U_1, ℬ = U_2U_3, 𝒞 = U_2U_4. All three pairwise unions are G. So G∖ℬ and G∖𝒞 are disjoint subsets of 𝒜, and 𝒜 ∩ ℬ ∩ 𝒞 = 𝒜 ∖ ((G∖ℬ) ⊔ (G∖𝒞)) (M-S Lemma 3.1, re-proved here). Hence
100:     |𝒜 ∩ ℬ ∩ 𝒞| = 1/2 − 1/4 − 1/4 = 0.
101:   - But U_2 ⊆ 𝒜 ∩ ℬ ∩ 𝒞, a contradiction. ∎
102: 
103: **F-3.** The Core(5) ⇒ Sun(5) direction holds group by group. So the minimal-order G over the full target carries a Core(5) configuration.
104: 
105: ## 3. Minimal counterexample
106: 
107: Suppose Sun(5) fails. Let G have minimal order among finite groups containing five pairwise disjoint cosets with all pairwise index-gcds ≤ 4. By §2, G carries a Core(5) configuration (U_i, g_i).
108: 
109: Every proper subgroup of G satisfies Sun(5). Every group satisfies Sun(k) for k ≤ 4: Sun 2006 Lemma 2.1 / L0.6 for k = 2, and Zhu Theorem 1.1 for k = 3, 4.
110: 
111: ## 4. Patterns, certificates, and why a certificate implies impossibility
112: 
113: **Patterns.** Put α_ij = [G:U_i∩U_j]/(4r_ir_j) ∈ {1, 2, 3} (L0.3). Let E2 be the vertex whose r is even and E3 the vertex whose r is divisible by 3, if they exist. Up to relabelling, (α, E2, E3) is one of 21,150 orbits. Burnside's lemma (Claude review v1 §1) confirms the count. Every rule is S5-equivariant, so one certificate per orbit suffices.
114: 
115: **Certificates.** The frozen program `code/core5_engine_v1.py` (65ce5a00…2eb1) gives, in `evidence/engine-v1-run1/certificates.tsv.gz` (1c7e8008…be85), one certificate for every orbit except S-D:
116: 
117: | rule | orbits |
118: |---|---|
119: | T | 20,465 |
120: | K | 637 |
121: | P-Bonferroni | 20 |
122: | N2 | 5 |
123: | FH2 | 22 |
124: 
125: **From certificate to impossibility.** Each certificate names a lemma of `rules-v1.md` together with its witness, and that lemma shows that no Core(5) configuration with that pattern exists. The program evaluates each triangle at τ = τ_min and uses representative r's. That suffices because of rules-v1 L8:
126: 
127: - **(M1)** Every admissible τ is a multiple of τ_min. Every rule is upward closed in τ:
128:   - a T-kill means τ_min > τ_max;
129:   - a K1 link at τ_min forces τ = τ_min;
130:   - the lower bounds in (P3) increase with τ, while hi and the K2 conditions do not depend on τ;
131:   - F, H2 and N2 do not read the program's τ.
132: 
133:   So a kill at τ_min is a kill for every admissible τ.
134: - **(M2)** All quantities depend on r only through the E2/E3 status, except the fibre gcds. Those are maximised by the representatives 16 and 81. So a kill at the representative is a kill for every instance.
135: 
136: **Certificate semantics** (Claude review v1 §3; sizes in units of |G|, everything recomputed from (α, E2, E3)):
137: 
138: - **T(a,b,c).**
139:   - τ_min is the lcm over the edges of α_e/γ_e, where γ_e = α_e if (α_e = 2 and the opposite vertex is E2) or (α_e = 3 and the opposite vertex is E3), and γ_e = 1 otherwise.
140:   - τ_max is the minimum, over the three vertices, of the product of the two α's at that vertex.
141:   - The certificate requires τ_min > τ_max.
142: - **K(v,S).** For every pair {i,j} ⊆ S, τ_min(v,i,j) = α_viα_vj; and Σ_{i∈S} α_iv ≥ 4.
143: - **PB / P-pair(v, ·).**
144:   - x_j = α_vj/4, and u = 1/(4γ) with γ = gcd(r_v, α_jk).
145:   - hi is the largest multiple of u that is ≤ min(x_j, x_k). It is lowered by u when the containment that equality would force is barred by K2.
146:   - PB: Σ_S(1 − x_j) − Σ_{pairs}(1 − x_j − x_k + hi_jk) ≥ 1.
147:   - P-pair: ⌈max(τ_min(v,j,k)/(4α_jk), x_j + x_k − 1)⌉_u > hi.
148:   - In run1 every P certificate is PB.
149: - **N2.** All ten α equal 2 (rules-v1 L5).
150: - **FH2(a,b).** Requires α_ab = 1 and V2 = {v : α_va = α_vb = 2} ≠ ∅. Both alternatives of rules-v1 L7-H2 are refuted for the same (a,b):
151:   - **case (i):** m = 4, t_a = t_b = 1, and t_v = 2 for v ∈ V2;
152:   - **case (ii):** m = 2, and t = 1 on {a, b} ∪ V2.
153: 
154:   In each case:
155:   - all other t_l range over 1..m;
156:   - every met-coset set M_l with |M_l| = t_l is enumerated;
157:   - the only symmetry used is fixing a's coset to label 0;
158:   - λ_l = 4r_lt_l/m;
159:   - an assignment is rejected iff (F3) fails at some vertex with t = 1, or some coset with s ≥ 2 has all pairwise gcds < s;
160:   - no coverage or balance constraint is imposed.
161: 
162:   The certificate requires every assignment to be rejected in both cases. Each of the 22 records lists the counts examined in both cases, [864, 16]. The Codex engine audit and the parent bridge confirm the engine implements exactly this.
163: 
164: **Verification status.**
165: - **Parent bridge replay: PASS.** This is a parent-independent replay of the 21,150 orbits and their certificates (R/sun-core5-parent-rule-audit-v1/), with verifier 2d7fff78…7659 and RESULT d741396c…7278.
166: - **C4** in engine-v1-run1 is a shared-code witness recheck. It is not independent verification.
167: - **check v2, lane SELF-REPORT.** `code/core5_check_v2.py` (9e4aac2a…48ae2) classifies all 2,125,764 labelled (α, E2, E3) triples. It reports 0 survivors, because it also encodes rule RSD, the Lemma R argument of §6. The 60 labelled triples it kills by RSD are exactly the labelled S-D triples: the S5-orbit of S-D, whose stabiliser is {id, (3 4)}. The parent bridge has no RSD rule, so it reports these same 60 as SURVIVE 60, and all its other labelled counts equal check v2's. The two classifications therefore agree. In both, the 60 S-D triples are disposed of by the written Lemma R argument in §6.
168: - **Controls** record observations only; they do not gate anything.
169: 
170: ## 5. Lemma R: right-coset fibres over a vertex subgroup [NEW]
171: 
172: **Setting.** G is finite. U_1, …, U_5 ≤ G have indices n_i. **Assume the cosets g_1U_1, …, g_5U_5 are pairwise disjoint (F-9).** Fix a vertex v and left-translate the whole configuration so that g_v = 1; disjointness is preserved. For j ≠ v put D_j := U_v g_j U_j, a (U_v, U_j)-double coset, and d_j := |D_j|/|U_j|.
173: 
174: **Statement.**
175: - **(R1)** D_j ∩ U_vU_j = ∅.
176: - **(R2)** |D_j| is a positive multiple of |G|/gcd(n_v, n_j).
177: - **(R3)** Every right coset U_vy contained in D_j meets g_jU_j. If w ∈ g_jU_j ∩ U_vy, then g_jU_j ∩ U_vy = (U_v ∩ wU_jw⁻¹)w, and [U_v : U_v ∩ wU_jw⁻¹] = d_j.
178: - **(R5′)** If j ≠ k and D_j ∩ D_k ≠ ∅, then gcd(d_j, d_k) ≥ 2.
179: - (R4) from earlier versions is deleted, since it is unused.
180: 
181: **Proof of (R1).** Since g_vU_v = U_v and g_jU_j are disjoint, L0.2 gives g_j ∉ U_vU_j. Now U_vU_j = U_v·1·U_j is the double coset of 1, and D_j is the double coset of g_j. Distinct double cosets are disjoint, so D_j ∩ U_vU_j = ∅.
182: 
183: **Proof of (R2).** By L0.4, |U_v g_j U_j| is a multiple of |G|/gcd([G:U_v], [G:U_j]). It is positive because g_j ∈ D_j.
184: 
185: **Proof of (R3).**
186: - Let U_vy ⊆ D_j. Then y = c g_j u with c ∈ U_v and u ∈ U_j. So U_vy = U_v g_j u contains g_ju, which lies in g_jU_j.
187: - For w ∈ g_jU_j ∩ U_vy we have g_jU_j = wU_j and U_vy = U_vw. Then x ∈ wU_j ∩ U_vw ⟺ xw⁻¹ ∈ wU_jw⁻¹ and xw⁻¹ ∈ U_v. So g_jU_j ∩ U_vy = (U_v ∩ wU_jw⁻¹)w.
188: - For the index: [U_v : U_v ∩ wU_jw⁻¹] = |U_v · wU_jw⁻¹| / |wU_jw⁻¹|. Right multiplication by w preserves size, so |U_v · wU_jw⁻¹| = |U_vwU_j|. Since w ∈ D_j, U_vwU_j = D_j. Hence the index is |D_j|/|U_j| = d_j.
189: 
190: **Proof of (R5′).**
191: - D_j and D_k are both left U_v-invariant, so D_j ∩ D_k is too. Being non-empty, it contains a whole right coset C = U_vy.
192: - By (R3), choose w_j ∈ g_jU_j ∩ C and w_k ∈ g_kU_k ∩ C. The two pieces are P_j = g_jU_j ∩ C = A w_j and P_k = g_kU_k ∩ C = B w_k, where A = U_v ∩ w_jU_jw_j⁻¹ and B = U_v ∩ w_kU_kw_k⁻¹. Their indices in U_v are d_j and d_k.
193: - P_j ∩ P_k = ∅, because g_jU_j ∩ g_kU_k = ∅.
194: - **Right-translate into U_v.** Multiply both pieces on the right by w_j⁻¹. This gives A and Bc, where c = w_kw_j⁻¹ lies in U_v because w_j and w_k lie in the same right coset C. So A and Bc are disjoint subsets of U_v.
195: - **Invert.** A⁻¹ = A and (Bc)⁻¹ = c⁻¹B. These are disjoint left cosets in U_v of subgroups of indices d_j and d_k.
196: - **Apply k = 2 (L0.6) in U_v.** If gcd(d_j, d_k) = 1, then AB = U_v, so c⁻¹ ∈ AB, i.e. c⁻¹ = ab with a ∈ A and b ∈ B. Then a = c⁻¹b⁻¹ ∈ A ∩ c⁻¹B, a contradiction.
197: - Hence gcd(d_j, d_k) ≥ 2. ∎
198: 
199: **Core(5) form.** In Core(5), gcd(n_v, n_j) = 4. So |D_j| = q_j |G|/4 with q_j a positive integer. By (R1), D_j ⊆ G ∖ U_vU_j, which has size (4 − α_vj)|G|/4, so 1 ≤ q_j ≤ 4 − α_vj. Then d_j = q_j|G|/4 ÷ (|G|/(4r_j)) = q_jr_j.
200: 
201: ## 6. Refutation of the pattern S-D
202: 
203: **S-D:**
204: - α01 = α02 = 1, α03 = α04 = 2, and α12 = α13 = α14 = α23 = α24 = α34 = 3;
205: - E2 = 1, so r_1 is even and 3 ∤ r_1;
206: - E3 = 0, so 3 | r_0 and r_0 is odd;
207: - r_2, r_3, r_4 are coprime to 6.
208: 
209: Assume a Core(5) configuration with this pattern. Take v = 2, translate so that g_2 = 1, and put X_j = U_2U_j for j ∈ {0, 1, 3, 4}.
210: 
211: **Step 1: sizes, and D_j = Y_j for j ∈ {1, 3, 4}.**
212: - By L0.3, |X_0| = α_20|G|/4 = |G|/4 and |X_1| = |X_3| = |X_4| = 3|G|/4.
213: - For j ∈ {1, 3, 4} put Y_j = G ∖ X_j, so |Y_j| = |G|/4.
214: - By (R1), D_j ⊆ Y_j. By (R2), |D_j| is a positive multiple of |G|/4.
215: - Hence D_j = Y_j, q_j = 1 and d_j = r_j.
216: 
217: **Step 2: Y_1, Y_3, Y_4 are pairwise disjoint.** If Y_j ∩ Y_k ≠ ∅ for distinct j, k ∈ {1, 3, 4}, then D_j ∩ D_k ≠ ∅. By (R5′), gcd(r_j, r_k) ≥ 2, contradicting coprimality.
218: 
219: **Step 3: X_0 ⊆ X_1 ∩ X_3 ∩ X_4.** Use rules-v1 L1 in the triangles {0, 1, 2}, {0, 2, 3} and {0, 2, 4}.
220: - In {0, 1, 2}: by (T2), τ ≤ α01·α02 = 1, so τ_012 = 1.
221: - In {0, 2, 3}: (T1) gives α03 = 2 | r_2 τ, and r_2 is odd, so τ is even. (T2) gives τ ≤ α02·α03 = 2. Hence τ_023 = 2. The same argument gives τ_024 = 2.
222: - By (T3) with apex 2:
223:   - |U_2(U_0∩U_1)| = τ_012|G|/(4α01) = |G|/4;
224:   - |U_2(U_0∩U_3)| = τ_023|G|/(4α03) = 2|G|/8 = |G|/4;
225:   - |U_2(U_0∩U_4)| = |G|/4.
226: - Each of these sets lies inside X_0 = U_2U_0, which has size |G|/4. So X_0 = U_2(U_0∩U_j) ⊆ U_2U_j = X_j for j = 1, 3, 4.
227: 
228: **Step 4: G ∖ X_0 = Y_1 ⊔ Y_3 ⊔ Y_4.**
229: - By Step 3, X_0 ∩ Y_j = ∅ for j = 1, 3, 4.
230: - By Step 2, |Y_1 ⊔ Y_3 ⊔ Y_4| = 3|G|/4 = |G ∖ X_0|.
231: 
232: **Step 5: the coset g_0U_0.**
233: - **Location.** By (R1), D_0 ⊆ G ∖ X_0. By (R2), |D_0| = q_0|G|/4 with q_0 ∈ {1, 2, 3}, and d_0 = q_0r_0.
234: - **D_0 meets some Y_j.** D_0 is non-empty, so by Step 4 it meets some Y_j = D_j with j ∈ {1, 3, 4}.
235: - **Gcd condition.** (R5′) gives gcd(q_0r_0, r_j) ≥ 2. Since gcd(r_0, r_j) = 1, gcd(q_0r_0, r_j) = gcd(q_0, r_j).
236:   - For j = 3, 4 the number r_j is coprime to 6, so gcd(q_0, r_j) = 1 for q_0 ∈ {1, 2, 3}. This is impossible.
237:   - So D_0 does not meet Y_3 or Y_4, and therefore D_0 ⊆ Y_1.
238: - **Contradiction (q_0 = 1 route).** Because |Y_1| = |G|/4, D_0 ⊆ Y_1 forces q_0 = 1. Then gcd(q_0, r_1) = gcd(1, r_1) = 1, which contradicts (R5′) for the pair {0, 1}.
239: - **Equivalent route (proof-v1).** gcd(q_0, r_1) ≥ 2 with r_1 even and 3 ∤ r_1 forces q_0 = 2, and then |D_0| = |G|/2 > |Y_1|.
240: 
241: So no configuration has the pattern S-D. ∎
242: 
243: ## 7. Closure of the proof
244: 
245: Suppose Sun(5) fails. By §3, a minimal-order group G carries a Core(5) configuration. Its pattern (α, E2, E3) lies, up to relabelling, in one of the 21,150 orbits.
246: 
247: - If the orbit is not S-D, its certificate (§4) shows that no Core(5) configuration with that pattern exists. This holds for all admissible τ (M1) and for all pairwise coprime r with the given E2/E3 status (M2).
248: - If the orbit is S-D, §6 refutes it.
249: 
250: So no counterexample exists. Sun's conjecture holds at k = 5 for every group, subject to the verification status recorded in §4. ∎
251:

1: # Rules v1: lemmas used by the Core(5) pattern engine
2: 
3: Lane: `mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/sun-coset5`. Created 2026-10-18 at about 09:35 PDT (host clock), then frozen read-only. Corrections go in a new version only.
4: 
5: Status of each item:
6: - **[PUB]**: published, cited exactly.
7: - **[PUB+]**: published, with a gap or misprint that is corrected here, and the correction is proved.
8: - **[NEW]**: new in this lane. The proof is written out and has **not been reviewed** by the parent or by a hostile reviewer.
9: 
10: Notation for the primary sources:
11: - **M-S** is Margolis–Schnabel, arXiv:1803.03569v1 (`sources/arxiv-1803.03569v1.pdf`). The journal version is Beitr. Algebra Geom. 60 (2019) 399–418.
12: - **Zhu** is arXiv:0807.2207v1 (`sources/arxiv-0807.2207v1.pdf`), Int. J. Mod. Math. 3 (2008) 197–206.
13: - **Sun** is arXiv:math/0501451v4 (`sources/arxiv-math0501451v4.pdf`), Internat. J. Math. 17 (2006) 1047–1064.
14: - **Menon** is arXiv:2608.15873v1 (`sources/arxiv-2608.15873v1.pdf`).
15: 
16: ## 0. Setting and notation
17: 
18: **Harmonic configuration.** G is a finite group. U_1, ..., U_k ≤ G are subgroups with n_i = [G:U_i], and g_1, ..., g_k ∈ G are elements such that the cosets g_iU_i are pairwise disjoint. Finiteness of G is justified by R0 in the README.
19: 
20: **Pairwise data.** For each pair i ≠ j put
21: - g_ij = gcd(n_i, n_j);
22: - I_ij = [G : U_i ∩ U_j];
23: - α_ij = I_ij / lcm(n_i, n_j). This is an integer, because n_i and n_j both divide I_ij.
24: 
25: **The Core(5) setting.**
26: - k = 5 and n_i = 4 r_i, where the r_i are pairwise coprime positive integers.
27: - E2 is the unique vertex with r_i even, if there is one. E3 is the unique vertex with 3 | r_i, if there is one. E2 = E3 is allowed.
28: - Consequently g_ij = 4, lcm(n_i, n_j) = 4 r_i r_j and I_ij = 4 r_i r_j α_ij.
29: 
30: **Minimal counterexample (used in F2).** Suppose Core(5) fails. Fix a counterexample in a group G of minimal order among all finite groups that contain five pairwise disjoint cosets of subgroups whose pairwise index-gcds are all ≤ 4. Then every proper subgroup of G satisfies Sun's statement for k = 5.
31: 
32: ## L0. Basic facts
33: 
34: **L0.1 [standard] Product formula.** For subgroups X, Y: |XY| = |X||Y| / |X ∩ Y|.
35: 
36: **L0.2 [PUB, M-S Lemma 3.2(3)].** gU ∩ hV = ∅ if and only if g⁻¹h ∉ UV.
37: 
38: *Proof.* gu = hv holds if and only if g⁻¹h = u v⁻¹ ∈ UV.
39: 
40: **L0.3.** In a harmonic configuration, |U_iU_j| = α_ij |G| / g_ij and 1 ≤ α_ij ≤ g_ij − 1. In Core(5) this reads α_ij ∈ {1, 2, 3} and |U_iU_j| = α_ij |G| / 4.
41: 
42: *Proof.*
43: - By L0.1, |U_iU_j| = |G| I_ij / (n_i n_j) = |G| lcm(n_i, n_j) α_ij / (n_i n_j) = α_ij |G| / g_ij.
44: - By L0.2 and disjointness, g_i⁻¹g_j ∉ U_iU_j. So U_iU_j ≠ G, which gives α_ij < g_ij.
45: 
46: **L0.4 [PUB, M-S Lemma 3.4(1)].** For subgroups A, B and any x ∈ G, |AxB| is a multiple of |G| / gcd([G:A], [G:B]).
47: 
48: *Proof.*
49: - |AxB| = |A||B| / |A ∩ xBx⁻¹| = |G| [G : A ∩ xBx⁻¹] / ([G:A][G:B]).
50: - [G : A ∩ xBx⁻¹] is a multiple of lcm([G:A], [G:B]).
51: - Substituting gives |AxB| = (integer) · |G| / gcd([G:A], [G:B]).
52: 
53: **L0.5 [PUB, mirror of M-S Lemma 3.4(2)].** For subgroups A, B, C, the set AB ∩ AC is a union of (A, B∩C)-double cosets.
54: 
55: *Proof.* Let x = ab = a'c, with y ∈ A and w ∈ B∩C. Then yxw = (ya)(bw) = (ya')(cw), which lies in AB ∩ AC.
56: 
57: **L0.6 [PUB, Sun 2006 Lemma 2.1 / M-S Lemma 2.2].** If [G:A] and [G:B] are coprime, then AB = G, so every left coset of A meets every left coset of B.
58: 
59: **L0.7 [PUB] Sun's statement for k ≤ 4, in every group.**
60: - k = 2: Sun 2006 Remark 2.2 (equivalently L0.6).
61: - k = 3, 4: Zhu Theorem 1.1 (Thms 2.2 and 3.1). It is also a consequence of M-S Theorem B.
62: 
63: **L0.8 [PUB, M-S Props 4.2, 4.3, 4.5]. Index tuples that are G-harmonic for no group G.** Throughout, the r_i are pairwise coprime.
64: - **Prop 4.2:** (2r_1, 2r_2, 2r_3).
65: - **Prop 4.3:** (3r_1, ..., 3r_4).
66: - **Prop 4.5:** (2r_1, 4r_2, 4r_3, 4r_4) with r_1 odd.
67: 
68: These are used only in R1 (README). The engine reproduces them as a control (§C below).
69: 
70: ## L1. Triangle lemma T [NEW form; subsumes M-S Lemma 3.10(a),(c) and Zhu (3.1), (3.4)]
71: 
72: Fix distinct vertices a, b, c. Put L = lcm(n_a, n_b, n_c) and T = [G : U_a∩U_b∩U_c].
73: 
74: **Statement.** L divides T. The quotient τ = T/L is a positive integer satisfying:
75: 
76: - **(T1)** I_xy divides L·τ for each pair {x, y} ⊆ {a, b, c}.
77: - **(T3)** For every labelling {x, y, z} = {a, b, c}:
78:   |U_y(U_x∩U_z)| = |G| · L · τ / (n_y · I_xz).
79: - **(T2)** For every labelling, |U_y(U_x∩U_z)| ≤ min(|U_yU_x|, |U_yU_z|).
80: 
81: *Proof.*
82: - The triple intersection lies in each U_x and in each U_x∩U_y. Hence every n_x divides T, so L | T. Likewise every I_xy divides T = Lτ, which is (T1).
83: - (T3) is L0.1 applied with |U_y| = |G|/n_y, |U_x∩U_z| = |G|/I_xz and |U_x∩U_y∩U_z| = |G|/T.
84: - (T2) holds because U_y(U_x∩U_z) ⊆ U_yU_x ∩ U_yU_z.
85: 
86: **Definitions.**
87: - τ_min is the least positive τ satisfying (T1). The set of solutions of (T1) is exactly the set of multiples of τ_min, since I_xy | Lτ ⟺ (I_xy / gcd(I_xy, L)) | τ.
88: - τ_max is the largest τ satisfying (T2) at all three apexes.
89: 
90: **T-kill.** If τ_min > τ_max, no harmonic configuration has these data.
91: 
92: **Core(5) form.**
93: - L = 4 r_a r_b r_c.
94: - (T1) ⟺ α_xy | r_z τ.
95: - (T3) reads |U_y(U_x∩U_z)| = τ |G| / (4 α_xz).
96: - (T2) ⟺ τ ≤ α_xy α_xz and τ ≤ α_yz α_xz.
97: 
98: So τ_max is the smallest product of two of the three α's. Also τ_min = lcm over the edges xy of α_xy / gcd(α_xy, r_z). Here gcd(α, r_z) = α exactly when α = 2 and z = E2, or α = 3 and z = E3; otherwise it is 1.
99: 
100: ## L2. Linking [NEW form of the step used in M-S 3.7, 3.8, 3.10(b)]
101: 
102: **Statement.** If |U_y(U_x∩U_z)| = |U_yU_x|, then U_yU_x ⊆ U_yU_z, and also U_xU_y ⊆ U_zU_y.
103: 
104: *Proof.*
105: - U_y(U_x∩U_z) is contained in U_yU_x and has the same size, so the two sets are equal. It is also contained in U_yU_z.
106: - Taking inverses of U_yU_x ⊆ U_yU_z gives U_xU_y ⊆ U_zU_y.
107: 
108: **Core(5) form.** The hypothesis holds exactly when τ = α_xy α_xz, the product of the two α's at x.
109: 
110: ## L3. Lemma K [NEW generalisation of M-S Lemma 3.5; subsumes Cor 3.6, Prop 3.8, Cor 3.9, Lemma 3.10(b), Cor 3.11]
111: 
112: **Statement.** Let v be a vertex and S a set of other vertices. Suppose that for every pair {i, j} ⊆ S,
113: 
114:   U_vU_i ⊆ U_jU_i   or   U_vU_j ⊆ U_iU_j.
115: 
116: Then Σ_{i∈S} |U_iU_v| ≤ |G| − |U_v| < |G|, that is, Σ_{i∈S} α_iv / g_iv < 1. In Core(5) this reads Σ_{i∈S} α_iv ≤ 3.
117: 
118: *Proof.*
119: 1. **The sets g_iU_iU_v (i ∈ S) are pairwise disjoint.** Suppose g_iu_iu = g_ju_ju' with u, u' ∈ U_v.
120:    - Then g_j⁻¹g_i = u_j u' u⁻¹ u_i⁻¹ ∈ U_jU_vU_i.
121:    - If U_vU_i ⊆ U_jU_i, then U_jU_vU_i ⊆ U_jU_i. This contradicts L0.2 for the disjoint pair g_jU_j, g_iU_i.
122:    - If instead U_vU_j ⊆ U_iU_j, then g_i⁻¹g_j ∈ U_iU_vU_j ⊆ U_iU_j, the same contradiction.
123: 2. **None of these sets meets g_vU_v.** Each g_iU_iU_v is right U_v-invariant. If it met g_vU_v it would contain g_v, say g_v = g_iu_iu. Then g_vu⁻¹ = g_iu_i lies in g_vU_v ∩ g_iU_i, which is empty.
124: 3. **Counting.** Summing the sizes |g_iU_iU_v| = |U_iU_v| = α_iv |G| / g_iv gives the claim.
125: 
126: **K1 (links).** The pair condition for {i, j} at v holds if |U_i(U_v∩U_j)| = |U_iU_v|, or if |U_j(U_v∩U_i)| = |U_jU_v|.
127: - By L2, the first equality gives U_vU_i ⊆ U_jU_i, and the second gives U_vU_j ⊆ U_iU_j.
128: - Core(5) form: τ_{ivj} = α_iv α_vj, where the product is of the two α's at v.
129: 
130: **K2.** If U_vU_j ⊆ U_vU_k (v, j, k distinct), then α_vj / g_vj + α_kj / g_kj < 1. In Core(5): α_vj + α_kj ≤ 3.
131: 
132: *Proof of K2.* Apply L3 at vertex j with S = {v, k}. Its pair condition requires U_jU_v ⊆ U_kU_v, which is the inverse of the hypothesis.
133: 
134: **Corrections to M-S.**
135: - *M-S Cor 3.11.* The printed proof asserts "by Lemma 3.10a) gcd(r_v, α(U,W)) = 1". With α(U,W) = α(V,W) that step does not follow, because 3.10(a) becomes trivial. The statement is still true whenever α(U,W) is 1 or a prime:
136:   - since r_u and r_v are coprime, gcd(r_v, α) = 1 or gcd(r_u, α) = 1;
137:   - in the second case apply 3.10(b) with U and V swapped; the quantity |VU| + |UW| equals |UV| + |VW|.
138: 
139:   In this lane the statement is an instance of K1 + L3: τ ≤ τ_max forces a link at the vertex shared by the two equal edges. This settles the screen caveat.
140: - *M-S Lemma 4.4.* The first bullet is misprinted. It should read "α(U_2, U_3) ≠ 2", and that is what its proof shows.
141: 
142: ## L4. Vertex count P [NEW form; subsumes the Lemma 3.1 counts in M-S Props 4.3/4.5 and screen N1]
143: 
144: Fix a vertex v. For each other vertex j put X_j = U_vU_j and Y_j = G ∖ X_j.
145: 
146: **Statement.**
147: - **(P1)** |X_j| = α_vj |G| / g_vj, and U_v ⊆ X_j.
148: - **(P2)** |X_j ∩ X_k| is a multiple of u_vjk = |G| / gcd(n_v, I_jk). In Core(5), u_vjk = |G| / (4 gcd(r_v, α_jk)).
149: - **(P3)** |X_j ∩ X_k| ≥ max(|U_v(U_j∩U_k)|, |X_j| + |X_k| − |G|). The first term is given by (T3).
150: - **(P4)** If |X_j ∩ X_k| = |X_j|, then X_j ⊆ X_k, so K2 must hold for that containment.
151: - **(P5)** For every set S of other vertices:
152:   Σ_{j∈S} |Y_j| − Σ_{j<k in S} |Y_j ∩ Y_k| < |G|,
153:   where |Y_j ∩ Y_k| = |G| − |X_j| − |X_k| + |X_j ∩ X_k|.
154: 
155: *Proof.*
156: - (P2): by L0.5, X_j ∩ X_k is a union of (U_v, U_j∩U_k)-double cosets. By L0.4, each has size a multiple of |G| / gcd(n_v, I_jk). In Core(5), gcd(4r_v, 4r_jr_kα_jk) = 4 gcd(r_v, α_jk), because gcd(r_v, r_jr_k) = 1.
157: - (P3): U_v(U_j∩U_k) ⊆ X_j ∩ X_k, and inclusion–exclusion gives the second bound.
158: - (P4): immediate from K2.
159: - (P5): this is the Bonferroni inequality. Also ∪_j Y_j ⊆ G ∖ U_v, which is a proper subset of G.
160: 
161: **How the engine uses P (sound).** For each pair {j, k}:
162: - hi_jk is the largest multiple of u that is ≤ min(|X_j|, |X_k|). It is lowered by one unit u when equality would force a containment that K2 forbids. If |X_j| = |X_k|, equality would force X_j = X_k, and then both K2 conditions must hold.
163: - lo_jk is (P3) rounded up to a multiple of u.
164: - If lo_jk > hi_jk the pattern is killed: **P-pair**.
165: - Otherwise the left side of (P5) is non-increasing in each |X_j ∩ X_k|. If (P5) already fails with every |X_j ∩ X_k| set to hi_jk, it fails for the true values, and the pattern is killed: **P-Bonferroni**.
166: 
167: ## L5. N2: all ten α = 2 is impossible in Core(5) [NEW, unreviewed]
168: 
169: *Proof.*
170: 1. **Every triangle has τ = 2.** Each edge has α = 2, and at most one vertex is E2. So by (T1) τ is even, and by (T2) τ ≤ 4.
171:    - If τ = 4, then (T2) holds with equality at every apex. So every vertex is linked for the other two (K1), and L3 at any vertex of the triangle gives 2 + 2 ≤ 3, which is false.
172:    - Hence τ = 2. Therefore [G : U_a∩U_b∩U_c] = 8 r_a r_b r_c for every triangle.
173: 2. **Fix a vertex v with r_v odd.** One exists, since at most one r is even. For the other vertices j put X_j = U_vU_j; then |X_j| = |G|/2.
174: 3. **Pairwise intersections.** For other vertices j ≠ k put Z_jk = U_v(U_j∩U_k).
175:    - By (T3), |Z_jk| = 2|G| / (4·2) = |G|/4, and Z_jk ⊆ X_j ∩ X_k.
176:    - By (P2), |X_j ∩ X_k| is a multiple of |G| / (4 gcd(r_v, 2)) = |G|/4.
177:    - |X_j ∩ X_k| ≠ |G|/2: otherwise X_j = X_k, and K2 would give 2 + 2 ≤ 3.
178:    - Hence X_j ∩ X_k = Z_jk.
179: 4. **The three Z's of a triple coincide.** Take distinct other vertices j, k, l and put W = U_j∩U_k∩U_l, so [G:W] = 8 r_j r_k r_l.
180:    - Q = [G : U_v ∩ W] is divisible by the three triangle indices 8 r_v r_j r_k, 8 r_v r_j r_l and 8 r_v r_k r_l. By coprimality, Q is divisible by 8 r_v r_j r_k r_l.
181:    - So |U_vW| = |G| Q / (4 r_v · 8 r_j r_k r_l) ≥ |G|/4.
182:    - But U_vW ⊆ Z_jk ∩ Z_jl ∩ Z_kl, and each Z has size |G|/4. Hence Z_jk = Z_jl = Z_kl = U_vW.
183: 5. **Contradiction.** The six sets Z among the four other vertices are therefore all one set Z with |Z| = |G|/4.
184:    - The four sets X_j ∖ Z are pairwise disjoint, each of size |G|/4.
185:    - So |∪_j X_j| = 4·|G|/4 + |G|/4 = 5|G|/4 > |G|, a contradiction.
186: 
187: ## L6. Fibre lemma F [NEW form; the case of H1 below subsumes screen N3]
188: 
189: Let H ≤ G with [G:H] = m. For each vertex l put t_l = [U_l : U_l∩H] and λ_l = [H : U_l∩H].
190: 
191: **Statement.**
192: - **(F1)** n_l t_l = m λ_l and 1 ≤ t_l ≤ m. The coset g_lU_l meets exactly t_l left cosets of H.
193: - **(F2)** Fix a left coset C of H and z ∈ C, and let S_C = {l : g_lU_l ∩ C ≠ ∅}.
194:   - For each l ∈ S_C, the set z⁻¹(g_lU_l ∩ C) is a left coset in H of U_l∩H. These cosets are pairwise disjoint.
195:   - Hence, if |S_C| = s ≥ 2, then gcd(λ_l, λ_l') ≥ s for some l ≠ l' in S_C. This uses L0.7 for s ≤ 4, and for s = 5 the minimality of G (§0; H is proper).
196: - **(F3)** If U_i ⊆ H, then |U_iU_l| ≤ |HU_l| = t_l |G| / m, that is, α_il / g_il ≤ t_l / m. In particular, if U_i, U_j ⊆ H then α_ij / g_ij ≤ 1/m.
197: 
198: *Proof.*
199: - (F1): [G : U_l∩H] = n_l t_l = m λ_l. The set g_lU_lH is a union of |U_lH| / |H| = t_l left H-cosets. Also t_l ≤ m, since distinct cosets of U_l∩H in U_l lie in distinct cosets of H.
200: - (F2): take y ∈ g_lU_l ∩ C. Then g_lU_l = yU_l and C = yH, so g_lU_l ∩ C = y(U_l∩H). Since z⁻¹y ∈ H, z⁻¹y(U_l∩H) is a left coset in H. Disjointness is preserved by translation.
201: - (F3): U_iU_l ⊆ HU_l, and |HU_l| = |H||U_l| / |H∩U_l| = t_l |H|.
202: 
203: ## L7. Sources of a subgroup H
204: 
205: **H1 [PUB, M-S Lemma 3.7].** Suppose [G:U] = a r_u, [G:V] = a r_v, [G:W] = a r_w with r_u, r_v, r_w pairwise coprime, and α(U,V) = α(U,W) = α(V,W) = 1. Then UV = VU = UW = WU = VW = WV is a subgroup of index a. It contains U, V and W. In Core(5), a = 4.
206: 
207: **H2 [NEW, unreviewed].** In Core(5), suppose α_ab = 1, and let V2 be the non-empty set of vertices v with α_va = α_vb = 2. Put W = ⟨U_a, U_b⟩. Then one of the following holds:
208: - **(i)** [G:W] = 4, W = U_aU_b, and t_v = [U_v : U_v∩W] = 2 for every v ∈ V2;
209: - **(ii)** [G:W] = 2, and U_v ⊆ W for every v ∈ V2.
210: 
211: *Proof.* Fix v ∈ V2 and work in the triangle {v, a, b}.
212: 1. **τ = 2.** By (T2), τ ≤ min(2·2, 2·1, 2·1) = 2. By (T1), 2 | r_b τ and 2 | r_a τ. Since r_a and r_b are not both even, τ is even. So τ = 2.
213: 2. **U_vU_a = U_vU_b.** By (T3), |U_v(U_a∩U_b)| = 2|G| / (4·1) = |G|/2 = |U_vU_a| = |U_vU_b|. Hence U_vU_a = U_v(U_a∩U_b) = U_vU_b. Call this set X.
214: 3. **X = U_vW.** X is right-invariant under U_a and under U_b, hence under W. Since U_v ⊆ X, this gives U_vW ⊆ X. Conversely X = U_vU_a ⊆ U_vW. So X = U_vW, and |X| = |G|/2 shows W ≠ G.
215: 4. **[G:W] ∈ {2, 4}.** [G:W] divides gcd(4r_a, 4r_b) = 4.
216: 5. **Index 4.** U_aU_b ⊆ W, and |U_aU_b| = |G|/4 = |W|, so W = U_aU_b. Also |U_vW| = t_v |W| = |G|/2, so t_v = 2.
217: 6. **Index 2.** |U_vW| = |G|/2 = |W|, so U_v ⊆ W.
218: 
219: W does not depend on v, so the same alternative holds for all v ∈ V2.
220: 
221: ## L8. Soundness of the engine's evaluation points [NEW]
222: 
223: **(M1) Evaluating at τ = τ_min is enough.** Every admissible τ is a multiple of τ_min, so τ ≥ τ_min. Every rule the engine uses is upward closed in each triangle's τ:
224: - T-kill means τ_min > τ_max.
225: - A link (K1) at τ_min means (T2) holds with equality there. Then τ = τ_min is forced, because a larger τ would violate (T2). So the links present at τ_min are present for every admissible τ.
226: - The lower bounds in (P3) increase with τ. The values hi_jk and the K2 conditions do not depend on τ.
227: - F, H1, H2 and N2 do not use the program's τ.
228: 
229: So a kill at τ_min is a kill for every admissible τ.
230: 
231: **(M2) Representative values of r are enough.** A Core(5) pattern (α, E2, E3) quantifies over all pairwise coprime r. The engine instead fixes
232: 
233:   r_i = p_i · 16^[i=E2] · 81^[i=E3],   with distinct primes p_i ∈ {5, 7, 11, 13, 17}.
234: 
235: Every quantity it uses depends on r only through the following:
236: - g_ij = 4;
237: - whether α_xy | r_z, and gcd(r_v, α_jk). Both depend only on the E2/E3 status.
238: - In F, gcd(λ_l, λ_l') = 2^min(v_2) · 3^min(v_3).
239:   - No prime p ≥ 5 is common to the two, since the r's are coprime, t ≤ 4 and m ∈ {2, 4}.
240:   - With v_2(r_E2) = 4 ≥ 3 and v_3(r_E3) = 4 ≥ 2, each min equals the largest value over all instances.
241:   - The F-kill condition "no pair reaches s" is monotone, so a kill at the representative is a kill for every instance.
242: 
243: ## Engine rule order and certificates
244: 
245: **Order.** Rules are applied in the order T, K, P-pair, P-Bonferroni, N2, F-H1, F-H2. The certificate of a pattern is the first rule that fires, together with its witness:
246: - T: the triangle;
247: - K: the vertex and S;
248: - P: the vertex and the pair or S;
249: - N2: none;
250: - F: the source of H and the number of fibre assignments examined, all of which are refuted.
251: 
252: Patterns killed only through N2 or H2 are flagged, because those lemmas are [NEW] and unreviewed.
253: 
254: **F enumeration.**
255: - For each vertex l not known to lie inside H, the engine enumerates t_l ∈ {1, ..., m} and the set M_l of met cosets with |M_l| = t_l.
256: - Known inside vertices: the triangle for H1, and {a, b} for H2. For these t = 1, and they are placed in all possible cosets.
257: - Constraints:
258:   - (F3), with respect to every vertex that has t = 1;
259:   - t_v = 2 (case i) or t_v = 1 (case ii) for v ∈ V2 under H2;
260:   - (F2) in every coset, using the maximal possible gcds (M2).
261: 
262: **Controls (§C, required by the contract).**
263: - (C1) The same engine with rules T, K and P only, run on the shapes (2,2,2), (3,3,3,3) and (2,4,4,4) with r_1 odd, must kill every pattern. That reproduces M-S Props 4.2, 4.3 and 4.5.
264: - (C2) Realisable configurations must survive when the engine is run on their exact data: Menon's A5 example (6,6,6,10,15), the C2×S4 example (4,6,6,6,6), all their 4-subconfigurations, and k cosets of one subgroup. This is a sanity control with explicit permutation groups, not gate evidence.
265: - (C3) Exact computations give α = 1 in cyclic groups and α ≤ 2 in abelian groups for indices of the form 4r. These are sanity controls.
266: - (C4) Mutating one label (an α value or the E2/E3 status) in a certificate must break that certificate for the stated reason.

13: 
14: **L9.** The two translations are correct (`SOURCE02:L31–51`).
15: 
16: For a left \(H\)-coset \(C\), choosing \(y_l\in g_lU_l\cap C\) gives the piece \(y_l(U_l\cap H)\). Common left translation places every piece in \(H\), without conjugating its subgroup, and its index is \(\lambda_l\).
17: 
18: For a right \(U_v\)-coset \(C\), the piece is
19: \[
20: A_jw_j,\qquad A_j=U_v\cap w_jU_jw_j^{-1}.
21: \]
22: Its index is
23: \[
24: [U_v:A_j]=|D_j|/|U_j|=q_jr_j.
25: \]
26: Common right translation followed by inversion gives disjoint **left** cosets in \(U_v\). Both operations are bijections. The index is independent of the chosen \(w_j\).
27: 
28: Applying Sun separately to **every subset** proves the hereditary condition. In \(U_v\), there are at most five pieces; Sun(6) is unnecessary. Six pieces occur only in the \(H\)-fibre setting, where properness and ambient minimality supply Sun(6).
29: 
30: The maxgcd calculation is actually exact for the stated markers (`SOURCE02:L62–66`). A shared prime must come from a \(q\)-factor, hence is 2 or 3. At most one cofactor contains each prime. Its contribution therefore saturates at the other endpoint’s \(q\)-valuation: at most two for 2 and one for 3. Class-1 E2 remains exact. Coincident markers cause no interference between prime valuations.
31: 
32: Thus L9 preserves every genuine FH5 assignment and every genuine RLP/RPLP support. Restricting supports to hereditary-allowed sets is justified.
33: 
34: **L10.** Every step passes (`SOURCE01:L9–17`; `SOURCE02:L75–81`).
35: 
36: Equality of the products makes \(X\) right-invariant under \(\langle U_j,U_k\rangle=W\), giving \(X=U_vW\). Since \(\mu(X)<1\), \(W\) is proper. Both subgroup inclusions imply
37: \[
38: [G:W]\mid\gcd(5r_j,5r_k)=5,
39: \]
40: so the index is five. Inclusion \(U_jU_k\subseteq W\), together with its measure \(\alpha_{jk}/5\), forces \(\alpha_{jk}=1\) and equality with \(W\). The final product formula gives \([U_v:U_v\cap W]=\alpha_{vj}\). No normality or distinct-subgroup assumption is hidden here.
41:

43: \[
44: \gcd(5r_v,5r_jr_k\alpha_{jk})
45:  =5\gcd(r_v,\alpha_{jk})=5.
46: \]
47: The T lower bound gives intersection measure at least \(1/5\); XE excludes equality of the two \(2/5\)-sets. Granularity then forces intersection measure exactly \(1/5\), and equality with \(Z_{jk}\).
48: 
49: The assertion about prime factors of \(\tau\) is **justified in this setting**, but its short proof should be inserted. Up to edge order, the neighbour triangles have:

51: | Edge labels | \(\tau_{\min}\) | T-admissible \(\tau\) |
52: |---|---:|---|
53: | 222 | 2 | 2, 4 |
54: | 224 | 2 or 4 | 2, 4, or just 4 |
55: | 244 | 4 | 4, 8 |
56: | 444 | 4 | 4, 8, 12, 16 |
57: 
58: For the last two rows, at most one edge can have its 4-part reduced by the opposite E2 marker; another 4 remains in the lcm. This covers both E2 classes and absence. Consequently every prime factor of every relevant \(\tau\) is 2 or 3.
59: 
60: Therefore odd \(r_v\) is automatically coprime to these \(\tau\)’s when \(v\ne E3\). When \(v=E3\), excluding divisibility by 3 suffices. E5 and primes \(\ge7\) in \(r_v\) introduce no exception. Condition (iv) cannot simply be dropped: a 444 triangle permits \(\tau=12\).

It is unreachable here because \(\tau_{\min}\) is a power of two, so the existing N26 predicate gains no invalid witnesses. Replace the whole sufficient test by:

65:

An actual type with \(A_C=\varnothing\) necessarily has \(S_C=\varnothing\), contributes nothing to \(\bigcup Y_j\), and can be omitted. Crucially, **empty \(S\) with nonempty \(A\) is included**.

77: \]

The containment bars use the correct K2 inequalities, including both directions at equal sizes. Using upper intersection bounds in Bonferroni lowers its expression, so a PB violation remains valid.

Summing the column inequalities proves weak duality directly. The Farkas argument is valid: an infeasibility direction has column coefficients at most zero and positive objective; positive rational scaling gives objective one while preserving the required inequalities (`SOURCE02:L154–166`).
